# Cylinder loop weights and planar nesting

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-M-0476` | `PM-237.3` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Cylinder-loop-weights-and-planar-nesting-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Cylinder-loop-weights-and-planar-nesting-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). ```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We determine the growth exponent, at every fixed positive loop fugacity, of the critical honeycomb partition function for disjoint polygons separating two prescribed markers on a balanced cylinder. At fugacity two the cylinder exponent is 1/6; the corresponding planar nesting exponent and middle-strip nesting exponent are 1/12.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We determine the growth exponent, at every fixed positive loop fugacity, of the critical honeycomb partition function for disjoint polygons separating two prescribed markers on a balanced cylinder. At fugacity two the cylinder exponent is 1/6; the corresponding planar nesting exponent and middle-strip nesting exponent are 1/12.

- **claim-cylinder-fugacity-exponent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-cylinder-exponent)): For every fixed $\chi>0$, along even $N$, $$Z_N(\chi)=N^{\tau(\chi)+o(1)},\qquad \tau(\chi)= \begin{cases} \displaystyle\frac16-\frac{2}{3\pi^2} \bigl(\arccos(\chi/2)\bigr)^2,&0<\chi\le2,\\[4pt] \displaystyle\frac16+\frac{2}{3\pi^2} \bigl(\operatorname{arcosh}(\chi/2)\bigr)^2,&\chi\ge2. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: Determines the growth exponent of the separating-polygon partition function on the balanced cylinder at each fixed positive fugacity.
- **claim-critical-walk-weight** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-02-critical)): If $c_n$ is the number of $n$-edge self-avoiding walks from a specified honeycomb vertex and $\mu=\lim_{n\to\infty}c_n^{1/n}$, then $\rho=\mu^{-1}$. Moreover, $c_n\rho^n=\exp(o(n))$ as an upper bound.
  - Explanation: Identifies the critical vertex activity with the reciprocal connective constant and bounds critical walk masses subexponentially.
- **claim-open-strip-polynomial-input** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-e-open-input)): Put $D(a,b)=a^2+b^2+2dab-d^2$ and define $$p_N(a_1,\ldots,a_N)= \prod_{i<j}\frac{D(a_i,a_j)}{a_i-a_j} \operatorname{Pf}\left[ \frac{(a_i-a_j)(a_i+a_j+\rho)}{D(a_i,a_j)}\right].$$ In odd size the matrix is augmented by a column of ones and its negative transpose; $p_0=p_1=1$. This is a symmetric polynomial of per-variable degree at most $N-1$, with leading coefficient $p_{N-1}$. There is a polynomial vacuum vector $W_N$ with empty component $p_N$ and the following properties, where $P_N=W_N/p_N$ is interpreted as a rational vector.* 1. *The local operators satisfy $$\begin{aligned} R_2(v)R_1(u+v)R_2(u)&=R_1(u)R_2(u+v)R_1(v),\\ R(u)R(-u)&=I,\qquad R(j\lambda)=S_jS_j^{\mathsf t},\\ R_2(u)R_1(u+j\lambda)(I\otimes S_j) &=(S_j\otimes I)\widehat R_j(u)\qquad(j=2,3), \end{aligned}$$ where $\widehat R_2(u)=R(u+\lambda)$ and $\widehat R_3(u)$ is the one-slot identity. Subscripts indicate the two consecutive slots on which $R$ acts. The vector $P_N$ satisfies adjacent braid covariance: $$P_N(\ldots,u_i,u_{i+1},\ldots) =R_i(u_{i+1}-u_i)P_N(\ldots,u_{i+1},u_i,\ldots).$$ At $u_{i+1}=u_i+j\lambda$ it equals $S_jP_{\rm red}$; for $j=2$ the pair is replaced by $u_i+\lambda$, and for $j=3$ it is deleted.* 2. *For $f_j=\cos(w+j\lambda)$ the scalar reductions are $$\begin{aligned} p(Y,f_0,f_6)&=p(Y)(f_0+f_6+\rho) \prod_{z\in Y}(z-f_{-6})(z-f_{12}),\\ p(Y,f_{-2},f_2)&=p(Y,f_0)(f_{-2}+f_2+\rho) \prod_{z\in Y}(z+f_0). \end{aligned}$$ They also hold with all shifts negated.* 3. *In the variables $e^{iu_k}$, $W_N$ is Laurent polynomial with exponents between $-(2N-2)$ and $2N-2$. Shifting $u_k$ by $\pi$ changes a component’s sign exactly when slot $k$ is occupied. In its last variable $y=u_N$, the vacant part is a polynomial in $b=a(y)$ of degree at most $N-1$ with leading coefficient $W_{N-1}$; the occupied part is $\sin y$ times a polynomial in $b$ of degree at most $N-2$.* 4. *These last-variable polynomials are the unique interpolants at $y_i=u_i+3\lambda$, $i<N$. Write $b_i=a(y_i)$, $d_i=a(u_i-3\lambda)$, $e_i=a(u_i+6\lambda)$, and let $\mathcal R_i$ move the inserted row $y_i$ from immediately after row $i$ to the last position by successive braid boxes. Their vector data are $$Y_i=(a(u_i)+b_i+\rho) \prod_{\substack{j<N\\j\ne i}}(a(u_j)-d_i)(a(u_j)-e_i) \mathcal R_iS_3W_{N-2}.$$ Thus, with $\ell_i(b)=\prod_{j<N,j\ne i}(b-b_j)/(b_i-b_j)$, $$\begin{aligned} W_N^{\rm vac}(b)&=W_{N-1}\prod_{i<N}(b-b_i) +\sum_{i<N}(Y_i)_{\rm vac}\ell_i(b),\\ W_N^{\rm occ}(y)&=\sin y\sum_{i<N} \frac{(Y_i)_{\rm occ}}{\sin y_i}\ell_i(a(y)). \end{aligned}$$ These identities hold at generic arguments and then by polynomial continuation. In each $W_{N-2}$ the interpolated and selected rows are omitted.
  - Explanation: Supplies the open-strip vacuum polynomial, its local identities, scalar reductions, degree bounds, and interpolation data.
- **claim-homogeneous-cylinder-vacuum** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-02-vacuum-polynomial)): There is a homogeneous vector $V_N$ of total degree $K_N^*$ whose empty component is the symmetric polynomial $$\label{eq:02-h-pfaffian} h_N(x_1,\ldots,x_N)= \prod_{i<j}\frac{D_0(x_i,x_j)}{x_i-x_j} \operatorname{Pf}\left[\frac{x_i^2-x_j^2}{D_0(x_i,x_j)}\right].$$ For odd $N$, augment the skew matrix by a last column of ones and a last row of minus ones; set $h_0=h_1=1$. In any slot $k$, the vacant part of $V_N$ is polynomial in $x_k$ of degree at most $N-1$, and its occupied part is $\sqrt{x_k}$ times a polynomial of degree at most $N-2$. In the last slot the leading vacant coefficient is $V_{N-1}$. The quotient $U_N=V_N/h_N$ has empty component one and satisfies the braid and splitting identities $$U_N(\ldots,u_i,u_{i+1},\ldots) =R_i(u_{i+1}-u_i)U_N(\ldots,u_{i+1},u_i,\ldots),\qquad U_N\big\|_{u_{i+1}=u_i+j\lambda}=S_jU_{\mathrm{red}},\quad j=2,3.$$ The merge replaces $(q^{-1}x,qx)$ by $x$; the deletion removes $(x,q^3x)$. Both $V_N$ and $U_N$ are cyclically covariant. Furthermore, $U_N$ is covariant under reversing the row order, reflecting the pairing, and replacing $u_i$ by $3\lambda-u_i$.
  - Explanation: Constructs a homogeneous periodic vacuum vector and its normalized quotient with braid, splitting, cyclic, and reflection covariance.
- **claim-algebraic-stationarity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-02-algebraic-stationarity)): Let $T(z)$ pass an auxiliary slot, vacant or occupied, through rows $1,\ldots,N$ with boxes $R(z-u_i)$ and close the auxiliary output to its input on the annulus. With every closed loop given weight zero, $$T(z)U_N=U_N$$ as a rational identity in the row and auxiliary parameters.
  - Explanation: Shows that the normalized vacuum is stationary for the annular transfer when closed loops have zero weight.
- **claim-fixed-cylinder-slab-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-02-periodic-slab)): For every such fixed cylinder there are constants $C_N<\infty$ and $0<r_N<1$ such that $b_M(i,j)\le C_Nr_N^M$ for all endpoint types and every $M\ge1$.
  - Explanation: Bounds spanning path masses exponentially in longitudinal width while the cylinder is fixed.
- **claim-physical-cylinder-vacuum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-02-cylinder-vacuum)): At physical row parameters $u_i\in\{\lambda,2\lambda\}$, the column $T(3\lambda)$ restricted to vectors with zero empty component has spectral radius less than one. Its unique stationary state with empty component one is the regular continuation of $U_N$. It equals the absolutely convergent sum of half-cylinder configurations with vacant far boundary. The right-half state is $U_N(3\lambda-u_1,\ldots,3\lambda-u_N)$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the algebraic vacuum with the unique normalized physical stationary state and an absolutely convergent half-cylinder sum.
- **claim-contraction-characterization** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-02-characterization)): The rational functions $Z_{X,Y}(\chi)$ are uniquely determined by the following properties, with $\chi$ fixed.* 1. *$H_{X,Y}=h_N(X,Y)^2Z_{X,Y}$ is polynomial of degree at most $2N-2$ in each argument, and $Z$ is symmetric within each group.* 2. *An ascending merge or deletion within either group leaves $Z$ unchanged after making the corresponding replacement or deletion.* 3. *Sending one argument to zero or infinity omits that site.* 4. *Sending $x\in X$ and $y\in Y$ to infinity at fixed generic ratio gives $$Z_{X,Y}\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{}% }Z_{\widehat X,\widehat Y}Z_{x,y}, \qquad Z_{x,y}=1+\chi(2c)^2\frac{xy}{(x+y)^2}.$$* 5. *At size zero, $Z=1$.* *All limits are rational limits at generic values of the remaining arguments. Hats mean omission of the indicated sites.
  - Explanation: Characterizes the separating-loop contraction by polynomial bounds, group symmetry, reductions, omission limits, paired scaling, and the empty value.
- **claim-finite-cylinder-label-sum** (construction, [source](#stmt-e-finite-cylinder)): For $\chi=q^{-3}\tau+q^3\tau^{-1}$ and $\tau\ne0$, $$\label{eq:02-finite-formula} \widetilde h_N^2Z_{X,Y}(\chi) =\sum_{\substack{l_i\in\{-1,0,1\}\\ \sum_{i\in X}l_i=\sum_{i\in Y}l_i=M}} \tau^M(-2c)^{\sum_i l_i^2} \prod_{i<j}Q^{e(i,j)}_{l_il_j}(x_i/x_j).$$ The sum includes all allowed integers $M$. Apparent singularities at colliding arguments are evaluated by continuation.
  - Explanation: Expresses the normalized cylinder partition function as a finite label sum with equal group imbalance and continuation at collisions.
- **claim-contour-sector-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-contour-sectors)): For the prescribed site rays, every $0<\varepsilon<1$, and every $\eta\in\mathbb C$, put $\chi=2\cos\eta$. Then $$\label{eq:02-contour-sectors} \widetilde h_N^2Z_{X,Y}(2\cos\eta) =(2d)^N\sum_{M=-\min(s,b)}^{\min(s,b)} (-1)^{sb+M}e^{i\eta M} [p_+^{s-M}p_-^{b+M}]I.$$ The coefficient integrals are absolutely convergent. The identity extends to $\varepsilon=1$ and to coincident real site offsets by continuation and dominated convergence of the fused integrands.
  - Explanation: Represents the finite contraction by absolutely convergent contour-sector integrals and permits the prescribed confluent limits.
- **claim-confluent-sector-signs** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-02-sector-signs)): At $\varepsilon=1$ with confluent real site offsets, extract constant signs from the site factors and from each full pair of integration variables, multiplying double constituents before doing so, so that the value at zero real difference is positive, or its leading quadratic coefficient is positive when it vanishes. Their logarithms then satisfy conjugation under $u\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}% \mapsto% \EndAccSupp{}% }-u$. They are regular on $\mathbb R$ except for same-species coincidences with singular part $2\log\|u-u'\|$, including double–double coincidences. At fixed far counts, multiply the extracted signs by the explicit prefactor $(-1)^{sb+M}$ in Equation <a href="#eq:02-contour-sectors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:02-contour-sectors">[eq:02-contour-sectors]</a>. The resulting sign has $M$-dependence exactly $(-1)^{fM}$, up to an $M$-independent sign, where $f=f_X+f_Y$ is the number of far elementary particles. The remaining explicit phase is $e^{i\eta M}$.
  - Explanation: Determines the sign-normalized singularities and the precise imbalance dependence of the residual sector sign.
- **claim-balanced-denominator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-balanced-denominator)): For $N=2m\ge2$, with exactly $m$ sites at each of $0,-i\beta$, the normalization $h_N$ is nonzero. As $N\to\infty$ through these even sizes, $$\label{eq:02-denominator} \log\left\| \frac{\widetilde h_N^2} {(\gamma/2)^N\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2} \right\| =\frac5{24}\log N+o(\log N).$$
  - Explanation: Proves nonvanishing and the logarithmic asymptotic of the normalization at balanced confluent sites.
- **claim-exact-mean-centering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-centering)): The particle–site kernel satisfies $V=H_{\cdot n}*\varrho$. Combining the site–site factors with $\exp((\mu,H\mu)/2)$ gives $$C_*^N\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2,\qquad C_*=\frac{H_*(0)}{\sqrt{B_s(0)}} =\frac{(1+d)\gamma}{4d\cos(\beta/2)},$$ with the fixed signs prescribed by the contour-sector formula. Here the logarithmic site coordinates $a_i$ are the balanced confluent sites: $m$ copies of $0$ and $m$ copies of $-i\beta$. The function $H_*$ is the removable analytic function used in Theorem <a href="#thm:02-denominator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:02-denominator">[thm:02-denominator]</a>.
  - Explanation: Centers the particle-site interaction exactly and extracts the site product and scalar self-centering factor.
- **claim-real-interaction-coercivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-coercivity)): Suppose that the components of $x$ are initially smooth rapidly decreasing real densities and that $\int d_x=0$. The tail $-w_Iw_J\|u\|$ contributes exactly $\\|D\\|_2^2$; residual real kernels are integrable. We have $$B(x)\asymp\\|D\\|_2^2+\sum_I\\|x_I\\|_{-1/2}^2,\qquad \\|x_I\\|_{-1/2}^2=\int(1+k^2)^{-1/2}\|\widehat x_I\|^2\,dk/(2\pi).$$ The form extends accordingly and controls $\\|D\\|_{1/2}^2=\int (1+k^2)^{1/2}\|\widehat D\|^2\,dk/(2\pi)$ also.
  - Explanation: Controls neutral real fluctuations by their cumulative weighted mass and species Sobolev norms.
- **claim-compensated-sector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-03-compensated-sector)): Fix an explicit configuration $\zeta$ with generic distinct real coordinates and a physical integer imbalance $M$ for which $m'_\sigma\ge0$. After division of the right-hand side of <a href="#eq:02-contour-sectors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:02-contour-sectors">[eq:02-contour-sectors]</a> by $(\gamma/2)^N\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2$, its contribution at $M,\zeta$ is $$c_\zeta\,e^{i(\eta+\pi f)M} \int A(X_{m'},\zeta)\exp\{-\Phi_M(X_{m'}-m'p_L)\}, \qquad A(X,\zeta)=\exp\{-(X,R_h\zeta)\}.$$ The integral includes the two *unnormalized* reference measures. The explicit integration measures and their species factorials are retained outside it. The prefactor $c_\zeta$ is independent of $M$, satisfies $\|c_\zeta\|\le\exp(C_hn_e)$, and uses the fixed sign convention of Lemma <a href="#lem:02-sector-signs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:02-sector-signs">11</a>. Moreover $0\le A\le1$. Sectors with a negative $m'_\sigma$ contribute zero.
  - Explanation: Rewrites each physical sector using smooth compensated costs, unnormalized reference measures, and a bounded explicit-particle prefactor.
- **claim-uniform-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-03-smoothing)): For the trial energy defined above, set $$x=\operatorname{Re}\alpha-\mu+\psi*\zeta,\quad z=\operatorname{Im}\alpha$$ with all species included for $x$, only near entries nonzero for $z$. These are weighted-total neutral. Then $$\operatorname{Re}\mathcal E(\alpha,\zeta) \ \ge\ B(x)-B(z)-(x,\operatorname{Im}H\,z) -s(h)(B(x)+B(z)+1)-C_h n_e,\qquad s(h)\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{}% }0. \label{eq:G-1}$$
  - Explanation: Compares the trial energy with smoothed real and imaginary quadratic forms while controlling regularization and omitted self interactions.
- **claim-explicit-particle-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-explicit-counts)): Constants may depend on the fixed smoothing parameter $h$. One has $$\|\operatorname{Re}M\|^2+n_e^2\le C_h L(B(x)+1),\qquad \ell_e:=\sum_{v\in\zeta} (\|v\|-L)_+\le C(B(x)+1). \label{eq:G-3}$$
  - Explanation: Controls imbalance, explicit variable count, and exterior excursion by the positive real energy.
- **claim-tilted-trial-action** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-03-trial-bounds)): Fix $a_0<\pi$ and $b_0<\infty$, and let $\eta=a+ib_1$ satisfy $\|a\|\le a_0$, $\|b_1\|\le b_0$. The following bounds hold uniformly for admissible real parts of $\alpha$, with the imaginary fields specified below. In parts 1–3, $h>0$ is fixed and sufficiently small.* 1. *If $M$ is real and $z=0$, then for sufficiently small fixed $h>0$, with no truncation of the explicit counts, $$\mathcal A\ge cB(x)-C_hL-C_hn_e.$$* 2. *Suppose $n_e$ is polynomially bounded, and that $p_L$ has the properties stated above. For any $v=\operatorname{Im}M\in\mathbb R$ and any $j\in\mathbb Z$, the choice $z_\sigma=-\sigma vp_L$ gives $$\mathcal A\ge cB(x)-C_hL-C_hn_e +2\pi jv-C_h\bigl(v^2+(1+f)\|v\|\bigr).$$ There is no polynomial restriction on $j$ in this estimate.* 3. *Suppose in addition that $\|j\|$ is polynomially bounded. There are a value $v=\operatorname{Im}M$, independent of $\operatorname{Re}M$, and an imaginary field $z$ with the required totals such that $$\mathcal A\ge cB(x)+c_\delta U^2L-C_hL-C_hn_e.$$ The value $v$ is polynomially bounded. Each $z_\sigma+\sigma vp_L$ is the derivative of a smooth primitive supported in $\|u\|\le(1-\delta/2)L$, with polynomial bounds on all required fixed-order derivatives. Moreover, the number of far particles in $[-(1-2\delta)L,(1-2\delta)L]$ is at most $CU$ for large $L$.* 4. *Under the same polynomial hypotheses, suppose $U\le U_0$. Choose a desired error $\varepsilon>0$, then $h$ sufficiently small, then $\delta$ sufficiently small. The constant $U_0$ may be fixed arbitrarily after these choices. There are a value $v=\operatorname{Im}M$, independent of $\operatorname{Re}M$, and an imaginary field $z$, with the same support and polynomial regularity properties as in part 3, such that $$\mathcal A\ge c'_\varepsilon B(x) +2L\frac{\operatorname{Re}(\eta^2)}{4\kappa} -C\varepsilon L-o(L)-C_hn_e.$$ The selected $v$ is polynomially bounded. The retained coefficient $c'_\varepsilon>0$ can be taken independent of $\delta,U_0$. On these bounded-$U$ configurations, integrating the explicit factorial measures with the factor $\exp(C_hn_e)$, while using a fixed portion of the retained $B(x)$-damping, costs at most $$\exp\bigl(C_{h,\varepsilon}\delta L+o(L)\bigr).$$ The same conclusion allows further fixed exponential factors per explicit particle after reducing $\delta$ as needed.* *The constants are uniform once the polynomial bounds and the previously fixed parameters are fixed. In part 4 the asymptotic limit is taken only after $\varepsilon,h,\delta,U_0$ have been chosen in that order; constants depending on those fixed choices are allowed in $o(L)$.
  - Explanation: Provides the untruncated real action bound and progressively sharper imaginary trial fields, including the quadratic tilt cost.
- **claim-compensated-kernel-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-03-kernel-regularity)): The kernel $K$ is smooth, extending evenly by reflection in each endpoint variable. $K$ and all derivatives of total order $l$ satisfy bounds $C^{l+1}(l!)^P(1+L)$, and $\mathscr K$ and its derivatives satisfy $C^{l+1}(l!)^P e^{-c\|s-v\|}$. For the latter estimates one only extends locally across endpoints. Here and below $\operatorname{poly}(L)$ prefactors may replace $1+L$ without harm.
  - Explanation: Shows that compensation removes the diagonal singularity and gives smooth reflected kernels with controlled derivatives and decay.
- **claim-compensated-source-regularity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-03-source-regularity)): Suppose $\|M\|,n_e,\ell_e$ have fixed polynomial bounds in $L$. In the $s$-coordinate, the linear source $$b=H_{nn}(m'p_L-\mu)+\widetilde H_{ne}\zeta$$ in $\Phi_M$ is smooth and even under endpoint reflection. Its derivatives of order $r$ are bounded by $\operatorname{poly}(L)C^{r+1}(r!)^P$, for a fixed Gevrey order $P$, and it has the core analytic continuation just described. Writing $$E_e=\tfrac12(\zeta,H\zeta)_{\rm off},$$ the difference $\mathcal E(m'p_L,\zeta)-E_e$ is polynomially bounded in absolute value. The unchanged explicit-pair term $E_e$, independent of $M$, has real part bounded below by a negative polynomial; no upper bound is required.
  - Explanation: Bounds the compensated source and constant term polynomially under polynomial restrictions on the explicit data and imbalance.
- **claim-relative-operator-spectrum** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-spectral-bounds)): On the complexification of $\mathcal X$, let $$T=I+j^*\mathscr K j, \qquad A_T=\tfrac12(T+T^*).$$ There are constants independent of $L$ such that $\\|T-I\\|_1\le CL$, $\\|T\\|\le C$, and $$A_T\ge cL^{-2}I, \qquad N_{A_T}(t):=\dim\mathbf1_{(-\infty,t)}(A_T) \le CL\sqrt t\quad(0<t<t_0). \label{eq:G-8}$$ The same lower gap and counting estimate hold for the singular values of $T$, and for every orthogonal compression of $T$.
  - Explanation: Gives trace and operator norm bounds, a positive Hermitian gap, and small-value counts for the relative operator and its compressions.
- **claim-relative-determinant-asymptotic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-determinant)): The Fredholm determinant satisfies $$-\tfrac12\log\|\det T\|=(7\gamma/8)L+o(L). \label{eq:G-9}$$
  - Explanation: Determines the leading contribution of the negative half logarithm of the relative determinant modulus.
- **claim-smooth-finite-core** (construction, [source](#stmt-e-finite-core)): For every sufficiently small fixed $\xi>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$, there are $\delta_*>0$, independent of $L$, and a real orthogonal projection $P$ in $\mathcal X$, of rank $O_\xi(L)$, whose range consists of smooth fields supported in $\|s\|\le(1-\delta_*)L$, such that $$\\|T-I-P(T-I)P\\|_1\le\xi L. \label{eq:G-10}$$ An orthonormal basis of its range can be chosen with every required fixed derivative bounded by a polynomial in $L$. The images of these basis fields under $h_0$ and $H_{\rm form}$, regarded as test densities for the $ds$ pairing, have the same bounds and smooth odd reflections at the endpoints. Constants may depend on $\xi$ and on the derivative order.
  - Explanation: Approximates the trace-class perturbation by a smooth interior core of controlled rank and derivative size.
- **claim-schur-determinant-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-schur-comparison)): Let $S=\operatorname{ran}P$, $R=S^\perp$, and $$T_e=T_{RR}-T_{RS}T_{SS}^{-1}T_{SR}.$$ The Hermitian part $\operatorname{Re}T_e=(T_e+T_e^*)/2$ has the gap and small-eigenvalue count in <a href="#eq:G-8" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-8">[eq:G-8]</a>. Moreover, $$\left\|\log\|\det T_{SS}\|-\log\|\det T\|\right\|=o_\xi(L), \qquad \sum_{\substack{\lambda<1\\ \lambda\in\operatorname{spec}(\operatorname{Re}T_e)}} -\log\lambda=o_\xi(L). \label{eq:G-11}$$ Eigenvalues are counted with multiplicity. Here the notation means $$\lim_{\xi\downarrow0}\limsup_{L\to\infty} \frac{\|o_\xi(L)\|}{L}=0.$$ It does not assert an $o(L)$ error for a fixed $\xi$. One may take errors bounded respectively by $C\xi^{1/5}L$ and $C\xi^{1/3}(1+\|\log\xi\|)L$, after the core approximation is made with tolerance $\xi$.
  - Explanation: Transfers positivity to the Schur complement and compares determinant costs in the iterated tolerance and size limits.
- **claim-circle-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-circle-comparison)): For a real smooth test $f(t)=f_0+2\sum_{k\ge1} f_k\cos kt$, with smooth even reflection, put $w_\pm=2(1\pm\cos t)$. Then $$J_n(w_+ e^f)J_n(w_- e^f)e^{-2n f_0}\le \exp\sum_{k\ge1} k \|f_k\|^2 . \label{eq:G-12}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the product of tilted reference integrals through the cosine coefficients of a smooth reflected real test.
- **claim-coarse-reference-source** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-reference-source)): For two independent normalized reference ensembles with dimensions $0\le\nu_\sigma\le C_0e^{\gamma L}$, $L\ge1$, and every real smooth reflected test $f_\sigma$, one has $$\label{eq:G-13} \mathbb E_0 e^{\ell(Q)} \le \prod_\sigma J_{\nu_\sigma}(w_0)^{-1} \exp\bigl(\\|\ell\\|_*^2/2\bigr) \le\exp\bigl(C L+\\|\ell\\|_*^2/2\bigr).$$ The constant depends only on $C_0$ and $\gamma$; a zero-dimensional ensemble consists of the empty configuration and has partition mass one.
  - Explanation: Bounds normalized source expectations at all allowed integer dimensions while retaining the reference partition masses.
- **claim-sharp-reference-source** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-sharp-reference-source)): If $\nu_\sigma\asymp m$, $\mathcal G=\{\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\le L^D\}$ for fixed $D$, and the tests arise from real smooth even-reflected $f_\sigma(s)$ with polynomial derivative bounds in the $s$-coordinate as specified above, then uniformly, $$\mathbb E_0[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G} e^{\ell(Q)}] \le \exp(o(L)+\\|\ell\\|_*^2/2) \label{eq:G-14}$$ (where the normalized excess limsup is at most zero). The error is uniform once $D$, the polynomial derivative bounds, and the constants implicit in $\nu_\sigma\asymp m$ are fixed.
  - Explanation: Removes the leading volume loss from the source bound on a controlled empirical-primitive event for comparable dimensions.
- **claim-compensated-quadratic-integration** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-quadratic-integration)): Let $F$ be a bounded real symmetric operator on $\mathcal X$, with $F\ge gI$, $g>0$, and let $F-I$ be trace class. Let $l\in\mathcal X$ be real. Suppose that there are a smooth real vector test $b(s)$, odd under endpoint reflection, and a smooth real matrix kernel $\mathscr C(s,v)$ satisfying $\mathscr C(s,v)=\mathscr C(v,s)^{\mathsf t}$, odd under reflection in either variable, such that $$h_0(l,Q)=\int b(s)^{\mathsf t}Q(s)\,ds,\qquad h_0(Q,(F-I)Q)=\iint Q(s)^{\mathsf t}\mathscr C(s,v)Q(v)\,ds\,dv.$$ Let $c\in\mathbb R$. For the compensated cost $$V(Q)=c+h_0(l,Q)+\tfrac12h_0(Q,(F-I)Q),\qquad m_F=c-\tfrac12h_0(l,F^{-1}l),$$ one has $$\log\mathbb E_0e^{-V(Q)} \le -m_F+CL+\tfrac12\sum_{\lambda<1,\ \lambda\in\operatorname{spec}(F)} -\log\lambda . \label{eq:G-18}$$ Here and below smooth compensated expressions are evaluated on empirical primitives by distributional pairing; their individual singular $h_0$-energies are not defined. For the sharp form used below, assume $\nu_\sigma\asymp m$, $g^{-1}+\\|F\\|+\\|l\\|_{\mathcal X}\le CL^a$ for fixed $C,a$, and polynomial physical derivative bounds for $b$ and $\mathscr C$ in the convention above. Then for every fixed $D$, <a href="#eq:G-18" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-18">[eq:G-18]</a> holds with $CL$ replaced by $o(L)$ and expectation restricted to $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\le L^D$. The error is uniform once these bounds and $D$ are fixed. The constant term $c$ is unrestricted: its factor $e^{-c}$ occurs on both sides of the estimate.
  - Explanation: Bounds quadratic perturbation expectations by the completed-square minimum and the negative logarithmic spectral cost, with a sharper restricted version.
- **claim-integer-dimension-sector-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-04-integer-bounds)): Fix $\|\operatorname{Re}\eta\|\le a_0<\pi$ and $\|\operatorname{Im}\eta\|\le b_0<\infty$, with the parameter conventions of Proposition <a href="#prop:03-trial-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:03-trial-bounds">18</a>. For nonnegative integer dimensions $\nu_\sigma$, put $$\mathcal L_{\nu,j}(M) =\log\|e^{i(\eta+\pi f+2\pi j)M}F_\nu(M)\|, \qquad v=\operatorname{Im}M.$$ The following estimates hold.* 1. *If $M$ is real and $0\le\nu_\sigma\le C e^{\gamma L}$, then, without any restriction on the explicit counts, $$\mathcal L_{\nu,j}(M)\le C_hL+C_hn_e-c_hd_e.$$ This bound is independent of the integer alias $j$.* 2. *Suppose $n_e$ is polynomially bounded, as in part 2 of Proposition <a href="#prop:03-trial-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:03-trial-bounds">18</a>. In the same dimension range, for every real $v$ and integer $j$, $$\mathcal L_{\nu,j}(M)\le C_h L+C_h n_e-c_h d_e-2\pi jv +C_h(v^2+(1+f)\|v\|). \label{eq:G-15}$$* 3. *Assume the common hypotheses of parts 3 and 4 of Proposition <a href="#prop:03-trial-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:03-trial-bounds">18</a>, polynomial bounds on $n_e,\|j\|,\|M\|,\ell_e$, and $\nu_\sigma\asymp m$. Choose the imaginary imbalance and trial density from part 3 of that proposition. Then $$\mathcal L_{\nu,j}(M)\le C_h L+C_h n_e-c_h d_e-c_\delta U^2 L+o(L). \label{eq:G-16}$$ If $U\le U_0$, the choice from part 4 instead gives $$\mathcal L_{\nu,j}(M)\le\frac{3\gamma L}{8} -2L\frac{\operatorname{Re}(\eta^2)}{4\kappa} +C\varepsilon L+o(L)+C_h n_e-c_{h,\varepsilon}d_e. \label{eq:G-17}$$ Here $\delta$ can be taken sufficiently small without degrading the displayed damping and per-particle constants, and $U_0$ may then be fixed arbitrarily, as in part 4 of that proposition.* *The estimates are uniform once the parameters in the trial bounds and the polynomial size powers are fixed.
  - Explanation: Turns deterministic action bounds into estimates for the actual reference integrals at integer dimensions.
- **claim-particle-contour-transport** (construction, [source](#stmt-e-particle-transport)): Under the hypotheses of part 3 of Proposition <a href="#prop:04-integer-bounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:04-integer-bounds">29</a>, choose the core projection $P$ of <a href="#eq:G-10" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-10">[eq:G-10]</a>, with $S=P\mathcal X$, $R=S^\perp$, and Schur complement $T_e$. For every prescribed fixed tail exponent, a sufficiently large fixed $D$ gives an exact contour deformation of the reference integral. On $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\le L^D$, its compensated cost is $C_0(Q+a(Q))-h_0(Q,Q)/2+o(1)$, where $$Q+a(Q)=iZ_0+Q_R+T_{SS}^{-1/2}Q_S -T_{SS}^{-1}Pu_z-T_{SS}^{-1}T_{SR}Q_R.$$ The extra Jacobian, after the one-coordinate reference-density cancellation, has modulus $(1+o(1))\|\det T_{SS}\|^{-1/2}$. The full cost $\operatorname{Re}C_0(Q+a(Q))$ has Hessian $I$ on $S$ and $\operatorname{Re}T_e$ on $R$, and its infimum is at least $m_z$. The complementary contribution is smaller than the prescribed stretched exponential.
  - Explanation: Realizes the affine fluctuation transformation by an exact deformation of the finite particle integration chain and computes its determinant factor.
- **claim-holomorphic-dimension-interpolation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-04-interpolation)): Fix polynomial bounds on $n_e,\ell_e,\|M\|$. For every fixed $b>0$ there is a jointly holomorphic function $\widehat F(\nu,M)$, with $\nu=(\nu_+,\nu_-)\in\mathbb C^2$, on a neighborhood of the indicated $M$-disk and the polydisk $\|\nu_\sigma-m\|\le\sqrt m$, such that $$\|\widehat F(\nu,M)\|\le \exp(L^C),\qquad \|\widehat F(\nu,M)-F_\nu(M)\|\le e^{-L^b} \quad\text{for }\nu\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^2\text{ in the polydisk}. \label{eq:G-20}$$ Here $C$ is fixed after the truncation bounds and $b$ are fixed. The bounds are uniform in the real explicit-particle positions on their labeled lines. The integrals include their reference masses $J_{\nu_+}(w_0)J_{\nu_-}(w_0)$, but no external phase factor.
  - Explanation: Constructs a holomorphic approximation to integer-dimensional reference integrals with prescribed grid accuracy and controlled complex growth.
- **claim-weighted-polynomial-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-04-hermite)): For the preceding Gaussian exponential, choose $a=1-\delta/2$. Given any fixed precision exponent, there is a polynomial $P(Y)$ of polynomial total degree $K_1$ such that $$\sup_{y\in\mathbb R^d}e^{-a\|y\|^2/2}\|g(y)-P(y)\|\le e^{-L^{B_1}}.$$ Its coefficients are holomorphic in $M$ and bounded by an exponential of a fixed polynomial. The degree and coefficient bounds are fixed before any endpoint expansion.
  - Explanation: Approximates the Gaussian exponential by a polynomial under a Gaussian weight, preserving holomorphic dependence and coefficient control.
- **claim-integer-grid-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-04-grid)): Suppose $M$ is in the fixed polynomial box and $\|F_\nu(M)\|\le B$ at all integer grid points of $\|\nu_\sigma-m\|\le\sqrt m$, with $B\ge e^{-L^{b'}}$, $b'<b$. If $\nu^*-m$ has polynomial size, then $$\|\widehat F(\nu^*,M)\|\le B\exp(o(L)). \label{eq:G-21}$$ The estimate is uniform under the stated bounds; in particular it applies to $\nu^*=m'(M)$.
  - Explanation: Transfers a sufficiently non-negligible integer-grid bound to complex dimensions at polynomial distance from the center.
- **claim-summed-contour-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-04-sector-sum)): Locally uniformly in the strip $\|\operatorname{Re}\eta\|<\pi$, $$\log \|G_N(\eta)\|\le 3\gamma L/8-2L\,\operatorname{Re}(\eta^2)/(4\kappa)+o(L) \label{eq:G-23}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the summed normalized contour representation locally uniformly throughout the stated complex strip.
- **claim-unbounded-fugacity-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-all-fugacity-bound)): There are constants $C<\infty$ and $N_0$ such that, for every even $N\ge N_0$ and every real $v\ge0$, $$Z_N(2\cosh v) \le \exp\!\bigl(C\log N\,(1+v^2)\bigr). \label{eq:06-all-fugacity}$$ The constants are independent of $v$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the physical partition function uniformly over the unbounded hyperbolic fugacity range.
- **claim-separating-count-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-11-polygon-tail)): There are constants $c,C>0$ such that for every sufficiently large even $N$ and every integer $a\ge0$, $$w_N(a)\le \exp\!\left(C\log N-\frac{ca^2}{\log N}\right). \label{eq:L-40}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds unnormalized coefficient masses with Gaussian decay in the separating-polygon count.
- **claim-annular-pinch-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-05-winding-data)): The function $\Lambda(\chi;z)$ is rational in $e^{2iz}$, with denominator dividing $\prod_i\mathscr D(z-b_i)$, where $\mathscr D$ is the local denominator in <a href="#eq:E-local-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:E-local-weights">[eq:E-local-weights]</a>, and tends to $1+\chi$ at both ends of this multiplicative coordinate. At the balanced specialization it is invariant under $z\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}% \mapsto% \EndAccSupp{}% }3\lambda-z$. For $u=b_i$ and generic remaining sites, $$\Lambda(\chi;u+j\lambda)\Lambda(\chi;u) =\begin{cases} \Lambda(\chi;u+\lambda),&j=2,\\ 1,&j=3. \end{cases} \label{eq:05-annular-pinch}$$ Set $w=2i(z-z_0)$ and, with derivatives evaluated at $\chi=0$, define $$\begin{gathered} f(w)=\partial_\chi\Lambda(\chi;z),\qquad h_2(w)=\partial_\chi^2\Lambda(\chi;z),\qquad g(w)=h_2(w)-f(w)^2,\\ (\mathscr L v)(w)=v(w+i\beta)+v(w-i\beta)-v(w). \end{gathered} \label{eq:05-pressure-derivatives}$$ Then $\mathscr L f$ and $\mathscr L g$ vanish to orders $2m,m,m$ at $0,i\beta,-i\beta$, respectively. The functions $f-1$ and $h_2$ are even rational functions of $e^w$, vanish at both real infinities, and have poles only at the translates of these three knots by $i\pi$, modulo $2\pi i$, with the same respective order bounds.
  - Explanation: Derives rationality, symmetry, pinches, and confluent zero and pole data for annular pressure derivatives.
- **claim-coercive-hardy-jet-interpolation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-05-hardy-interpolation)): For every fixed $0<a<1$, the operators $D_a$ are bounded and satisfy $$\operatorname{Re}D_a(k)\ge c_a>0 \quad(k\in\mathbb R),\qquad \\|(P_ND_a\|_{P_NH})^{-1}\\|\le c_a^{-1}. \label{eq:05-coercivity}$$ Moreover, writing $\\|\cdot\\|_{\partial,p}$ for the sum of the two boundary $L^p$ norms, one has $$\\|e^{aw}(1-f)\\|_{L^p(\operatorname{Im}w=y)} \le C_a e^{aA_N}, \qquad p=2,4,\quad \|y\|\le h+\beta. \label{eq:05-first-derivative-norms}$$ For fixed $0<a<1/2$, $$\bigl\|\mathscr Lf(s)\bigr\|+ \bigl\|\mathscr Lg(s)\bigr\| \le C_a\|B_N(s)\|\bigl(1+e^{2a(A_N-s)}\bigr), \qquad s\ge0. \label{eq:05-jet-residual}$$ For $\mathscr Lf$ alone, $2a$ can be replaced by any fixed $a\in(0,1)$.
  - Explanation: Obtains uniform inverse bounds and boundary and residual estimates for the winding-pressure interpolation problem.
- **claim-winding-pressure-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-05-pressure-bounds)): There are constants $0<c<C<\infty$ such that, for all sufficiently large even $N$, $$\frac cN\le f(0)\le\frac CN, \qquad \|g(0)\|\le\frac CN. \label{eq:05-winding-pressure}$$ In fact $e^{\gamma A_N}f(0)$ has a finite strictly positive limit.
  - Explanation: Bounds the winding-pressure derivatives at the physical point and proves a strictly positive limiting first-derivative coefficient.
- **claim-essential-polygon-removal** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-05-winding-suppression)): On the balanced cylinder with period $V=iN\sqrt3/2$, let $[\ell]$ range over simple essential polygons modulo integer horizontal translations, and set $W(\ell)=\rho^{\|\ell\|}$. If $d_x(\ell)$ is their horizontal span, then $$\sum_{[\ell]}W(\ell)\asymp N^{-1},\qquad \sum_{[\ell]}W(\ell)d_x(\ell)\le C. \label{eq:05-winding-moment}$$ The total weight of essential polygons separating the two marked points in the balanced two-marker geometry is bounded uniformly in $N$. At each fixed nonnegative fugacity, deleting all these polygons changes its partition function by at most a bounded multiplicative factor.
  - Explanation: Controls essential polygon mass and horizontal-span moment, yielding bounded deletion cost in the balanced marker geometry.
- **claim-variable-loop-local-algebra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-05-variable-algebra)): With $\widehat P=P_{x+\lambda}$ for $j=2$, and the identity for $j=3$, $$\begin{gathered} R_yR_{-y}=I,\qquad P_{j\lambda}=S_jS_j^{\mathsf t},\\ (P_x)_2(P_{x+j\lambda})_1(I\otimes S_j) =d_j(x)(S_j\otimes I)\widehat P,\qquad d_j(x)=1+nq_j\frac{A_{x+j\lambda}U_x} {e_{x+j\lambda}e_x}. \end{gathered} \label{eq:05-variable-splitting}$$ The raw boxes also satisfy the three-box Yang–Baxter identity.
  - Explanation: Establishes local transfer identities with the scalar corrections produced by nonzero closed-loop weight.
- **claim-open-transfer-pinches** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-05-open-pinch)): The matrices $t(z)$ commute and are invariant under $z\leftrightarrow\bar z$. At $\lambda=\lambda_0$, $x_i=\lambda/2$, and $z=3\lambda/2$, this is the transfer of two physical columns with vacant auxiliary boundaries. Its simple eigenvalue continuing $1$ will be denoted by $\Lambda_o(n;z)$. It is rational and $\pi$-periodic in $z$, equals one at $n=0$, and tends to $1+n$ at both imaginary infinities. For $u=x_i$, $v=u+j\lambda$, its pinch rule is $$\Lambda_o(n;v)\Lambda_o(n;u) =c_bc_t\prod_kd_j(u-x_k)d_j(\bar v-x_k) \begin{cases} \Lambda_o(n;u+\lambda),&j=2,\\ 1,&j=3, \end{cases} \label{eq:05-open-pinch}$$ where $w_0=u+v-3\lambda$ and $$c_b=\frac{e_{w_0}+nq_j\mathfrak m(\bar u)A_{w_0}}{L_{w_0}}, \qquad c_t=\frac{e_{-w_0}+nq_j\mathfrak m(v)A_{-w_0}}{L_{-w_0}}. \label{eq:05-boundary-scalars}$$
  - Explanation: Proves open-transfer commutation and reflection symmetry and records the eigenvalue pinch rule with boundary and bulk scalars.
- **claim-open-pressure-interpolation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-05-open-interpolation)): For some fixed $a\in(\gamma/2,1)$, and uniformly in $N$, $$\|Y(s)\|\le C e^{-cNe^{-\gamma s}} (1+N^{a/\gamma}e^{-as}),\qquad s\ge0. \label{eq:05-open-error}$$ Consequently $$\mathcal F_o(0)=(\rho_* *(N\phi+b))(0)+O(N^{-1}). \label{eq:05-open-center}$$
  - Explanation: Controls the forced open-strip interpolation residual and evaluates the center pressure derivative with an inverse-width error.
- **claim-linear-open-strip-pressure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-05-open-pressure)): For the polygon sum $Q_N$ defined at the start of this section, there are constants $c_1,c_2$ such that $$Q_N:=\sum_{[P]}\rho^{\|P\|}(N-H(P))_+ =c_1N+c_2+O(N^{-1}). \label{eq:05-strip-polygon-pressure}$$
  - Explanation: Determines the strip polygon pressure through its linear term, constant correction, and inverse-width remainder.
- **claim-planar-polygon-diameter-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-polygon-tail)): Uniformly for $R\ge0$, $$\sum_{[P]:\,\operatorname{diam}(P)>R}\rho^{\|P\|} \le\frac{C}{(1+R)^2}. \label{eq:05-planar-tail}$$ The sum is over unrooted, unoriented polygons modulo triangular-lattice translations.
  - Explanation: Bounds the diameter tail of critical polygon shapes modulo the full triangular translation lattice.
- **claim-macroscopic-polygon-removal** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-05-macroscopic-removal)): Fix $c_0>0$. In an $N$-band strip, the total weight of polygons enclosing a specified face center and having diameter greater than $c_0N$ is bounded by a constant depending only on $c_0$. The same is true of contractible polygons enclosing a specified marker on the balanced cylinder, with diameter measured on a planar lift. At each fixed nonnegative fugacity, deleting these polygons changes a nesting partition function by at most a bounded multiplicative factor.
  - Explanation: Controls large enclosing polygons in strips and balanced cylinders and bounds their deletion cost.
- **claim-planar-nesting-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-planar-nesting)): For every fixed $\chi\ge0$, and every sufficiently small fixed $c_0>0$, $$P_\chi(c_0N)^2 \le Z_N(\chi) \le C_{\chi,c_0}P_\chi(c_0N)^2 \label{eq:05-cylinder-planar-comparison}$$ for all sufficiently large even $N$. Consequently, for every fixed $\chi>0$, $$P_\chi(r)=r^{\tau(\chi)/2+o(1)}\qquad(r\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{}% }\infty), \label{eq:05-planar-all-fugacity}$$ where $\tau$ is the exponent in Theorem <a href="#thm:04-cylinder-exponent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:04-cylinder-exponent">[thm:04-cylinder-exponent]</a>. In particular, $$P_2(r)=r^{1/12+o(1)}\qquad(r\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{}% }\infty). \label{eq:05-planar-nest-scaling}$$ Here $P_\chi(r)$ includes the empty nest, and the diameter cutoff applies separately to every polygon in a nest.
  - Explanation: Compares the cylinder partition function to independent planar nests up to bounded factors and derives the planar growth exponent.
- **claim-middle-strip-nesting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-e-strip-nesting)): As $k\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{}% }\infty$, $$S_k=k^{1/12+o(1)}. \label{eq:C-nest}$$
  - Explanation: Determines the fugacity-two nesting exponent around a middle face of a strip.
- **claim-length-nest-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-length-nest)): Let $f_1,f_2$ be the two honeycomb face centers adjacent to $p$. Then $$d\bigl(\mathcal Z_{f_1}(D)+\mathcal Z_{f_2}(D)\bigr) \le L_p(D)\le \mathcal Z_{f_1}(D)+\mathcal Z_{f_2}(D).$$ The inequalities also hold, with finite quantities, in every homogeneous infinite strip of fixed positive height.
  - Explanation: Compares the winding-weighted visit sum at an internal mid-edge with nest masses around its adjacent faces.
- **claim-raw-strip-first-length-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-e-raw-first-moment)): There is a constant $C$, independent of the boundary source and $N\ge1$, such that $$\mathcal M_N(a)\le C(1+NS_{N+1}) \le N^{13/12+o(1)} \qquad(N\mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}% \longrightarrow% \EndAccSupp{}% }\infty). \label{eq:05-raw-first-moment}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the unnormalized first-length mass of paths from a fixed boundary source through the strip nesting mass.
- **claim-conditioned-strip-path-length** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-02-length-reduction)): Give $\mathcal P_N(a)$ its normalized critical weights. Conditional on the path reaching the top boundary of the bottom $\lfloor N/4\rfloor$ bands, its expected number of visited vertices is $N^{4/3+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Determines the expected critical path length conditional on reaching the stated macroscopic height.

        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"thm:04-cylinder-exponent\" label=\"thm:04-cylinder-exponent\"></span>For every fixed $\\chi>0$, along even $N$, $$Z_N(\\chi)=N^{\\tau(\\chi)+o(1)},\\qquad\n \\tau(\\chi)=\n \\begin{cases}\n \\displaystyle\\frac16-\\frac{2}{3\\pi^2}\n              \\bigl(\\arccos(\\chi/2)\\bigr)^2,&0<\\chi\\le2,\\\\[4pt]\n \\displaystyle\\frac16+\\frac{2}{3\\pi^2}\n              \\bigl(\\operatorname{arcosh}(\\chi/2)\\bigr)^2,&\\chi\\ge2.\n \\end{cases}$$",
      "explanation": "Determines the growth exponent of the separating-polygon partition function on the balanced cylinder at each fixed positive fugacity.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-cylinder-exponent",
          "exact_quote": "along even $N$,",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-cylinder-fugacity-exponent",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-cylinder-exponent",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\chi>0$,",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-e-cylinder-exponent"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-02-critical",
        "exact_quote": "If $c_n$ is the number of $n$-edge self-avoiding walks from a specified honeycomb vertex and $\\mu=\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}c_n^{1/n}$, then $\\rho=\\mu^{-1}$. Moreover, $c_n\\rho^n=\\exp(o(n))$ as an upper bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-02-critical",
          "exact_quote": "If $c_n$ is the number of $n$-edge self-avoiding walks from a specified honeycomb vertex and $\\mu=\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}c_n^{1/n}$, then $\\rho=\\mu^{-1}$. Moreover, $c_n\\rho^n=\\exp(o(n))$ as an upper bound.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $c_n$ is the number of $n$-edge self-avoiding walks from a specified honeycomb vertex and $\\mu=\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}c_n^{1/n}$, then $\\rho=\\mu^{-1}$. Moreover, $c_n\\rho^n=\\exp(o(n))$ as an upper bound.",
      "explanation": "Identifies the critical vertex activity with the reciprocal connective constant and bounds critical walk masses subexponentially.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-02-critical",
          "exact_quote": "If $c_n$ is the number of $n$-edge self-avoiding walks from a specified honeycomb vertex and $\\mu=\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}c_n^{1/n}$,",

        }

      "id": "claim-critical-walk-weight",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-02-critical"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-e-open-input",
        "exact_quote": "Put $D(a,b)=a^2+b^2+2dab-d^2$ and define $$p_N(a_1,\\ldots,a_N)=\n \\prod_{i<j}\\frac{D(a_i,a_j)}{a_i-a_j}\n \\operatorname{Pf}\\left[\n  \\frac{(a_i-a_j)(a_i+a_j+\\rho)}{D(a_i,a_j)}\\right].$$ In odd size the matrix is augmented by a column of ones and its negative transpose; $p_0=p_1=1$. This is a symmetric polynomial of per-variable degree at most $N-1$, with leading coefficient $p_{N-1}$. There is a polynomial vacuum vector $W_N$ with empty component $p_N$ and the following properties, where $P_N=W_N/p_N$ is interpreted as a rational vector.*\n\n1.  *The local operators satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n    R_2(v)R_1(u+v)R_2(u)&=R_1(u)R_2(u+v)R_1(v),\\\\\n    R(u)R(-u)&=I,\\qquad R(j\\lambda)=S_jS_j^{\\mathsf t},\\\\\n    R_2(u)R_1(u+j\\lambda)(I\\otimes S_j)\n      &=(S_j\\otimes I)\\widehat R_j(u)\\qquad(j=2,3),\n    \\end{aligned}$$ where $\\widehat R_2(u)=R(u+\\lambda)$ and $\\widehat R_3(u)$ is the one-slot identity. Subscripts indicate the two consecutive slots on which $R$ acts. The vector $P_N$ satisfies adjacent braid covariance: $$P_N(\\ldots,u_i,u_{i+1},\\ldots)\n     =R_i(u_{i+1}-u_i)P_N(\\ldots,u_{i+1},u_i,\\ldots).$$ At $u_{i+1}=u_i+j\\lambda$ it equals $S_jP_{\\rm red}$; for $j=2$ the pair is replaced by $u_i+\\lambda$, and for $j=3$ it is deleted.*\n\n2.  *For $f_j=\\cos(w+j\\lambda)$ the scalar reductions are $$\\begin{aligned}\n     p(Y,f_0,f_6)&=p(Y)(f_0+f_6+\\rho)\n                      \\prod_{z\\in Y}(z-f_{-6})(z-f_{12}),\\\\\n     p(Y,f_{-2},f_2)&=p(Y,f_0)(f_{-2}+f_2+\\rho)\n                      \\prod_{z\\in Y}(z+f_0).\n    \\end{aligned}$$ They also hold with all shifts negated.*\n\n3.  *In the variables $e^{iu_k}$, $W_N$ is Laurent polynomial with exponents between $-(2N-2)$ and $2N-2$. Shifting $u_k$ by $\\pi$ changes a component’s sign exactly when slot $k$ is occupied. In its last variable $y=u_N$, the vacant part is a polynomial in $b=a(y)$ of degree at most $N-1$ with leading coefficient $W_{N-1}$; the occupied part is $\\sin y$ times a polynomial in $b$ of degree at most $N-2$.*\n\n4.  *These last-variable polynomials are the unique interpolants at $y_i=u_i+3\\lambda$, $i<N$. Write $b_i=a(y_i)$, $d_i=a(u_i-3\\lambda)$, $e_i=a(u_i+6\\lambda)$, and let $\\mathcal R_i$ move the inserted row $y_i$ from immediately after row $i$ to the last position by successive braid boxes. Their vector data are $$Y_i=(a(u_i)+b_i+\\rho)\n        \\prod_{\\substack{j<N\\\\j\\ne i}}(a(u_j)-d_i)(a(u_j)-e_i)\n           \\mathcal R_iS_3W_{N-2}.$$ Thus, with $\\ell_i(b)=\\prod_{j<N,j\\ne i}(b-b_j)/(b_i-b_j)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     W_N^{\\rm vac}(b)&=W_{N-1}\\prod_{i<N}(b-b_i)\n                              +\\sum_{i<N}(Y_i)_{\\rm vac}\\ell_i(b),\\\\\n     W_N^{\\rm occ}(y)&=\\sin y\\sum_{i<N}\n                           \\frac{(Y_i)_{\\rm occ}}{\\sin y_i}\\ell_i(a(y)).\n    \\end{aligned}$$ These identities hold at generic arguments and then by polynomial continuation. In each $W_{N-2}$ the interpolated and selected rows are omitted.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-02-vacuum-polynomial",
        "exact_quote": "There is a homogeneous vector $V_N$ of total degree $K_N^*$ whose empty component is the symmetric polynomial $$\\label{eq:02-h-pfaffian}\n h_N(x_1,\\ldots,x_N)=\n \\prod_{i<j}\\frac{D_0(x_i,x_j)}{x_i-x_j}\n \\operatorname{Pf}\\left[\\frac{x_i^2-x_j^2}{D_0(x_i,x_j)}\\right].$$ For odd $N$, augment the skew matrix by a last column of ones and a last row of minus ones; set $h_0=h_1=1$. In any slot $k$, the vacant part of $V_N$ is polynomial in $x_k$ of degree at most $N-1$, and its occupied part is $\\sqrt{x_k}$ times a polynomial of degree at most $N-2$. In the last slot the leading vacant coefficient is $V_{N-1}$. The quotient $U_N=V_N/h_N$ has empty component one and satisfies the braid and splitting identities $$U_N(\\ldots,u_i,u_{i+1},\\ldots)\n =R_i(u_{i+1}-u_i)U_N(\\ldots,u_{i+1},u_i,\\ldots),\\qquad\n U_N\\big|_{u_{i+1}=u_i+j\\lambda}=S_jU_{\\mathrm{red}},\\quad j=2,3.$$ The merge replaces $(q^{-1}x,qx)$ by $x$; the deletion removes $(x,q^3x)$. Both $V_N$ and $U_N$ are cyclically covariant. Furthermore, $U_N$ is covariant under reversing the row order, reflecting the pairing, and replacing $u_i$ by $3\\lambda-u_i$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-02-vacuum-polynomial",
          "exact_quote": "There is a homogeneous vector $V_N$ of total degree $K_N^*$ whose empty component is the symmetric polynomial $$\\label{eq:02-h-pfaffian}\n h_N(x_1,\\ldots,x_N)=\n \\prod_{i<j}\\frac{D_0(x_i,x_j)}{x_i-x_j}\n \\operatorname{Pf}\\left[\\frac{x_i^2-x_j^2}{D_0(x_i,x_j)}\\right].$$ For odd $N$, augment the skew matrix by a last column of ones and a last row of minus ones; set $h_0=h_1=1$. In any slot $k$, the vacant part of $V_N$ is polynomial in $x_k$ of degree at most $N-1$, and its occupied part is $\\sqrt{x_k}$ times a polynomial of degree at most $N-2$. In the last slot the leading vacant coefficient is $V_{N-1}$. The quotient $U_N=V_N/h_N$ has empty component one and satisfies the braid and splitting identities $$U_N(\\ldots,u_i,u_{i+1},\\ldots)\n =R_i(u_{i+1}-u_i)U_N(\\ldots,u_{i+1},u_i,\\ldots),\\qquad\n U_N\\big|_{u_{i+1}=u_i+j\\lambda}=S_jU_{\\mathrm{red}},\\quad j=2,3.$$ The merge replaces $(q^{-1}x,qx)$ by $x$; the deletion removes $(x,q^3x)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-02-cylinder-vacuum",
        "exact_quote": "At physical row parameters $u_i\\in\\{\\lambda,2\\lambda\\}$, the column $T(3\\lambda)$ restricted to vectors with zero empty component has spectral radius less than one. Its unique stationary state with empty component one is the regular continuation of $U_N$. It equals the absolutely convergent sum of half-cylinder configurations with vacant far boundary. The right-half state is $U_N(3\\lambda-u_1,\\ldots,3\\lambda-u_N)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-02-characterization",
        "exact_quote": "The rational functions $Z_{X,Y}(\\chi)$ are uniquely determined by the following properties, with $\\chi$ fixed.*\n\n1.  *$H_{X,Y}=h_N(X,Y)^2Z_{X,Y}$ is polynomial of degree at most $2N-2$ in each argument, and $Z$ is symmetric within each group.*\n\n2.  *An ascending merge or deletion within either group leaves $Z$ unchanged after making the corresponding replacement or deletion.*\n\n3.  *Sending one argument to zero or infinity omits that site.*\n\n4.  *Sending $x\\in X$ and $y\\in Y$ to infinity at fixed generic ratio gives $$Z_{X,Y}\\mathrel{%\n      \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}%\n      \\longrightarrow%\n      \\EndAccSupp{}%\n    }Z_{\\widehat X,\\widehat Y}Z_{x,y},\n     \\qquad Z_{x,y}=1+\\chi(2c)^2\\frac{xy}{(x+y)^2}.$$*\n\n5.  *At size zero, $Z=1$.*\n\n*All limits are rational limits at generic values of the remaining arguments. Hats mean omission of the indicated sites.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-finite-cylinder",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:02-finite-formula\" label=\"thm:02-finite-formula\"></span>For $\\chi=q^{-3}\\tau+q^3\\tau^{-1}$ and $\\tau\\ne0$, $$\\label{eq:02-finite-formula}\n \\widetilde h_N^2Z_{X,Y}(\\chi)\n =\\sum_{\\substack{l_i\\in\\{-1,0,1\\}\\\\\n           \\sum_{i\\in X}l_i=\\sum_{i\\in Y}l_i=M}}\n       \\tau^M(-2c)^{\\sum_i l_i^2}\n       \\prod_{i<j}Q^{e(i,j)}_{l_il_j}(x_i/x_j).$$ The sum includes all allowed integers $M$. Apparent singularities at colliding arguments are evaluated by continuation.",

        "anchor": "stmt-e-contour-sectors",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:02-contour-sectors\" label=\"thm:02-contour-sectors\"></span>For the prescribed site rays, every $0<\\varepsilon<1$, and every $\\eta\\in\\mathbb C$, put $\\chi=2\\cos\\eta$. Then $$\\label{eq:02-contour-sectors}\n \\widetilde h_N^2Z_{X,Y}(2\\cos\\eta)\n =(2d)^N\\sum_{M=-\\min(s,b)}^{\\min(s,b)}\n       (-1)^{sb+M}e^{i\\eta M}\n       [p_+^{s-M}p_-^{b+M}]I.$$ The coefficient integrals are absolutely convergent. The identity extends to $\\varepsilon=1$ and to coincident real site offsets by continuation and dominated convergence of the fused integrands.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-02-sector-signs",
        "exact_quote": "At $\\varepsilon=1$ with confluent real site offsets, extract constant signs from the site factors and from each full pair of integration variables, multiplying double constituents before doing so, so that the value at zero real difference is positive, or its leading quadratic coefficient is positive when it vanishes. Their logarithms then satisfy conjugation under $u\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%\n  \\mapsto%\n  \\EndAccSupp{}%\n}-u$. They are regular on $\\mathbb R$ except for same-species coincidences with singular part $2\\log|u-u'|$, including double–double coincidences. At fixed far counts, multiply the extracted signs by the explicit prefactor $(-1)^{sb+M}$ in Equation <a href=\"#eq:02-contour-sectors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:02-contour-sectors\">[eq:02-contour-sectors]</a>. The resulting sign has $M$-dependence exactly $(-1)^{fM}$, up to an $M$-independent sign, where $f=f_X+f_Y$ is the number of far elementary particles. The remaining explicit phase is $e^{i\\eta M}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The resulting sign has $M$-dependence exactly $(-1)^{fM}$, up to an $M$-independent sign, where $f=f_X+f_Y$ is the number of far elementary particles.         "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:02-denominator\" label=\"thm:02-denominator\"></span>For $N=2m\\ge2$, with exactly $m$ sites at each of $0,-i\\beta$, the normalization $h_N$ is nonzero.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-centering",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-centering\" label=\"lem:03-centering\"></span>The particle–site kernel satisfies $V=H_{\\cdot n}*\\varrho$. Combining the site–site factors with $\\exp((\\mu,H\\mu)/2)$ gives $$C_*^N\\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2,\\qquad\n C_*=\\frac{H_*(0)}{\\sqrt{B_s(0)}}\n     =\\frac{(1+d)\\gamma}{4d\\cos(\\beta/2)},$$ with the fixed signs prescribed by the contour-sector formula. Here the logarithmic site coordinates $a_i$ are the balanced confluent sites: $m$ copies of $0$ and $m$ copies of $-i\\beta$. The function $H_*$ is the removable analytic function used in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:02-denominator\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:02-denominator\">[thm:02-denominator]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-e-coercivity",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-coercivity\" label=\"lem:03-coercivity\"></span>Suppose that the components of $x$ are initially smooth rapidly decreasing real densities and that $\\int d_x=0$. The tail $-w_Iw_J|u|$ contributes exactly $\\|D\\|_2^2$; residual real kernels are integrable. We have $$B(x)\\asymp\\|D\\|_2^2+\\sum_I\\|x_I\\|_{-1/2}^2,\\qquad\n \\|x_I\\|_{-1/2}^2=\\int(1+k^2)^{-1/2}|\\widehat x_I|^2\\,dk/(2\\pi).$$ The form extends accordingly and controls $\\|D\\|_{1/2}^2=\\int (1+k^2)^{1/2}|\\widehat D|^2\\,dk/(2\\pi)$ also.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-03-compensated-sector",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an explicit configuration $\\zeta$ with generic distinct real coordinates and a physical integer imbalance $M$ for which $m'_\\sigma\\ge0$. After division of the right-hand side of <a href=\"#eq:02-contour-sectors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:02-contour-sectors\">[eq:02-contour-sectors]</a> by $(\\gamma/2)^N\\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2$, its contribution at $M,\\zeta$ is $$c_\\zeta\\,e^{i(\\eta+\\pi f)M}\n \\int A(X_{m'},\\zeta)\\exp\\{-\\Phi_M(X_{m'}-m'p_L)\\},\n \\qquad A(X,\\zeta)=\\exp\\{-(X,R_h\\zeta)\\}.$$ The integral includes the two *unnormalized* reference measures. The explicit integration measures and their species factorials are retained outside it. The prefactor $c_\\zeta$ is independent of $M$, satisfies $|c_\\zeta|\\le\\exp(C_hn_e)$, and uses the fixed sign convention of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:02-sector-signs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:02-sector-signs\">11</a>. Moreover $0\\le A\\le1$. Sectors with a negative $m'_\\sigma$ contribute zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-03-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "For the trial energy defined above, set $$x=\\operatorname{Re}\\alpha-\\mu+\\psi*\\zeta,\\quad z=\\operatorname{Im}\\alpha$$ with all species included for $x$, only near entries nonzero for $z$. These are weighted-total neutral. Then $$\\operatorname{Re}\\mathcal E(\\alpha,\\zeta)\n \\ \\ge\\ B(x)-B(z)-(x,\\operatorname{Im}H\\,z)\n -s(h)(B(x)+B(z)+1)-C_h n_e,\\qquad s(h)\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{}%\n}0.\n \\label{eq:G-1}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-237.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-explicit-counts",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"cor:03-counts\" label=\"cor:03-counts\"></span>Constants may depend on the fixed smoothing parameter $h$. One has $$|\\operatorname{Re}M|^2+n_e^2\\le C_h L(B(x)+1),\\qquad\n \\ell_e:=\\sum_{v\\in\\zeta} (|v|-L)_+\\le C(B(x)+1).\n \\label{eq:G-3}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-explicit-counts",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"cor:03-counts\" label=\"cor:03-counts\"></span>Constants may depend on the fixed smoothing parameter $h$. One has $$|\\operatorname{Re}M|^2+n_e^2\\le C_h L(B(x)+1),\\qquad\n \\ell_e:=\\sum_{v\\in\\zeta} (|v|-L)_+\\le C(B(x)+1).\n \\label{eq:G-3}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"cor:03-counts\" label=\"cor:03-counts\"></span>Constants may depend on the fixed smoothing parameter $h$. One has $$|\\operatorname{Re}M|^2+n_e^2\\le C_h L(B(x)+1),\\qquad\n \\ell_e:=\\sum_{v\\in\\zeta} (|v|-L)_+\\le C(B(x)+1).\n \\label{eq:G-3}$$",
      "explanation": "Controls imbalance, explicit variable count, and exterior excursion by the positive real energy.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-03-trial-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $a_0<\\pi$ and $b_0<\\infty$, and let $\\eta=a+ib_1$ satisfy $|a|\\le a_0$, $|b_1|\\le b_0$. The following bounds hold uniformly for admissible real parts of $\\alpha$, with the imaginary fields specified below. In parts 1–3, $h>0$ is fixed and sufficiently small.*\n\n1.  *If $M$ is real and $z=0$, then for sufficiently small fixed $h>0$, with no truncation of the explicit counts, $$\\mathcal A\\ge cB(x)-C_hL-C_hn_e.$$*\n\n2.  *Suppose $n_e$ is polynomially bounded, and that $p_L$ has the properties stated above. For any $v=\\operatorname{Im}M\\in\\mathbb R$ and any $j\\in\\mathbb Z$, the choice $z_\\sigma=-\\sigma vp_L$ gives $$\\mathcal A\\ge cB(x)-C_hL-C_hn_e\n           +2\\pi jv-C_h\\bigl(v^2+(1+f)|v|\\bigr).$$ There is no polynomial restriction on $j$ in this estimate.*\n\n3.  *Suppose in addition that $|j|$ is polynomially bounded. There are a value $v=\\operatorname{Im}M$, independent of $\\operatorname{Re}M$, and an imaginary field $z$ with the required totals such that $$\\mathcal A\\ge cB(x)+c_\\delta U^2L-C_hL-C_hn_e.$$ The value $v$ is polynomially bounded. Each $z_\\sigma+\\sigma vp_L$ is the derivative of a smooth primitive supported in $|u|\\le(1-\\delta/2)L$, with polynomial bounds on all required fixed-order derivatives. Moreover, the number of far particles in $[-(1-2\\delta)L,(1-2\\delta)L]$ is at most $CU$ for large $L$.*\n\n4.  *Under the same polynomial hypotheses, suppose $U\\le U_0$. Choose a desired error $\\varepsilon>0$, then $h$ sufficiently small, then $\\delta$ sufficiently small. The constant $U_0$ may be fixed arbitrarily after these choices. There are a value $v=\\operatorname{Im}M$, independent of $\\operatorname{Re}M$, and an imaginary field $z$, with the same support and polynomial regularity properties as in part 3, such that $$\\mathcal A\\ge c'_\\varepsilon B(x)\n           +2L\\frac{\\operatorname{Re}(\\eta^2)}{4\\kappa}\n           -C\\varepsilon L-o(L)-C_hn_e.$$ The selected $v$ is polynomially bounded. The retained coefficient $c'_\\varepsilon>0$ can be taken independent of $\\delta,U_0$. On these bounded-$U$ configurations, integrating the explicit factorial measures with the factor $\\exp(C_hn_e)$, while using a fixed portion of the retained $B(x)$-damping, costs at most $$\\exp\\bigl(C_{h,\\varepsilon}\\delta L+o(L)\\bigr).$$ The same conclusion allows further fixed exponential factors per explicit particle after reducing $\\delta$ as needed.*\n\n*The constants are uniform once the polynomial bounds and the previously fixed parameters are fixed. In part 4 the asymptotic limit is taken only after $\\varepsilon,h,\\delta,U_0$ have been chosen in that order; constants depending on those fixed choices are allowed in $o(L)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-03-kernel-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "The kernel $K$ is smooth, extending evenly by reflection in each endpoint variable. $K$ and all derivatives of total order $l$ satisfy bounds $C^{l+1}(l!)^P(1+L)$, and $\\mathscr K$ and its derivatives satisfy $C^{l+1}(l!)^P e^{-c|s-v|}$. For the latter estimates one only extends locally across endpoints. Here and below $\\operatorname{poly}(L)$ prefactors may replace $1+L$ without harm.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For the latter estimates one only extends locally across endpoints. Here and below $\\operatorname{poly}(L)$ prefactors may replace $1+L$ without harm.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-03-source-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $|M|,n_e,\\ell_e$ have fixed polynomial bounds in $L$. In the $s$-coordinate, the linear source $$b=H_{nn}(m'p_L-\\mu)+\\widetilde H_{ne}\\zeta$$ in $\\Phi_M$ is smooth and even under endpoint reflection. Its derivatives of order $r$ are bounded by $\\operatorname{poly}(L)C^{r+1}(r!)^P$, for a fixed Gevrey order $P$, and it has the core analytic continuation just described. Writing $$E_e=\\tfrac12(\\zeta,H\\zeta)_{\\rm off},$$ the difference $\\mathcal E(m'p_L,\\zeta)-E_e$ is polynomially bounded in absolute value. The unchanged explicit-pair term $E_e$, independent of $M$, has real part bounded below by a negative polynomial; no upper bound is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-spectral-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-spectral\" label=\"lem:03-spectral\"></span>On the complexification of $\\mathcal X$, let $$T=I+j^*\\mathscr K j,\n \\qquad A_T=\\tfrac12(T+T^*).$$ There are constants independent of $L$ such that $\\|T-I\\|_1\\le CL$, $\\|T\\|\\le C$, and $$A_T\\ge cL^{-2}I,\n \\qquad N_{A_T}(t):=\\dim\\mathbf1_{(-\\infty,t)}(A_T)\n       \\le CL\\sqrt t\\quad(0<t<t_0).\n \\label{eq:G-8}$$ The same lower gap and counting estimate hold for the singular values of $T$, and for every orthogonal compression of $T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-spectral-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-spectral\" label=\"lem:03-spectral\"></span>On the complexification of $\\mathcal X$, let $$T=I+j^*\\mathscr K j,\n \\qquad A_T=\\tfrac12(T+T^*).$$ There are constants independent of $L$ such that $\\|T-I\\|_1\\le CL$, $\\|T\\|\\le C$, and $$A_T\\ge cL^{-2}I,\n \\qquad N_{A_T}(t):=\\dim\\mathbf1_{(-\\infty,t)}(A_T)\n       \\le CL\\sqrt t\\quad(0<t<t_0).\n \\label{eq:G-8}$$ The same lower gap and counting estimate hold for the singular values of $T$, and for every orthogonal compression of $T$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-e-finite-core",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-core\" label=\"lem:03-core\"></span>For every sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$, there are $\\delta_*>0$, independent of $L$, and a real orthogonal projection $P$ in $\\mathcal X$, of rank $O_\\xi(L)$, whose range consists of smooth fields supported in $|s|\\le(1-\\delta_*)L$, such that $$\\|T-I-P(T-I)P\\|_1\\le\\xi L.\n \\label{eq:G-10}$$ An orthonormal basis of its range can be chosen with every required fixed derivative bounded by a polynomial in $L$. The images of these basis fields under $h_0$ and $H_{\\rm form}$, regarded as test densities for the $ds$ pairing, have the same bounds and smooth odd reflections at the endpoints. Constants may depend on $\\xi$ and on the derivative order.",

        "anchor": "stmt-e-schur-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-schur\" label=\"lem:03-schur\"></span>Let $S=\\operatorname{ran}P$, $R=S^\\perp$, and $$T_e=T_{RR}-T_{RS}T_{SS}^{-1}T_{SR}.$$ The Hermitian part $\\operatorname{Re}T_e=(T_e+T_e^*)/2$ has the gap and small-eigenvalue count in <a href=\"#eq:G-8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-8\">[eq:G-8]</a>. Moreover, $$\\left|\\log|\\det T_{SS}|-\\log|\\det T|\\right|=o_\\xi(L),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\lambda<1\\\\\n        \\lambda\\in\\operatorname{spec}(\\operatorname{Re}T_e)}}\n       -\\log\\lambda=o_\\xi(L).\n \\label{eq:G-11}$$ Eigenvalues are counted with multiplicity. Here the notation means $$\\lim_{\\xi\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{L\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{|o_\\xi(L)|}{L}=0.$$ It does not assert an $o(L)$ error for a fixed $\\xi$. One may take errors bounded respectively by $C\\xi^{1/5}L$ and $C\\xi^{1/3}(1+|\\log\\xi|)L$, after the core approximation is made with tolerance $\\xi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-schur-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-schur\" label=\"lem:03-schur\"></span>Let $S=\\operatorname{ran}P$, $R=S^\\perp$, and $$T_e=T_{RR}-T_{RS}T_{SS}^{-1}T_{SR}.$$ The Hermitian part $\\operatorname{Re}T_e=(T_e+T_e^*)/2$ has the gap and small-eigenvalue count in <a href=\"#eq:G-8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-8\">[eq:G-8]</a>. One may take errors bounded respectively by $C\\xi^{1/5}L$ and $C\\xi^{1/3}(1+|\\log\\xi|)L$, after the core approximation is made with tolerance $\\xi$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-reference-source",
        "exact_quote": "For two independent normalized reference ensembles with dimensions $0\\le\\nu_\\sigma\\le C_0e^{\\gamma L}$, $L\\ge1$, and every real smooth reflected test $f_\\sigma$, one has $$\\label{eq:G-13}\n \\mathbb E_0 e^{\\ell(Q)}\n \\le \\prod_\\sigma J_{\\nu_\\sigma}(w_0)^{-1}\n       \\exp\\bigl(\\|\\ell\\|_*^2/2\\bigr)\n \\le\\exp\\bigl(C L+\\|\\ell\\|_*^2/2\\bigr).$$ The constant depends only on $C_0$ and $\\gamma$; a zero-dimensional ensemble consists of the empty configuration and has partition mass one.",

        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:03-uniform-source\" label=\"thm:03-uniform-source\"></span>If $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$, $\\mathcal G=\\{\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D\\}$ for fixed $D$, and the tests arise from real smooth even-reflected $f_\\sigma(s)$ with polynomial derivative bounds in the $s$-coordinate as specified above, then uniformly, $$\\mathbb E_0[\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal G} e^{\\ell(Q)}]\n \\le \\exp(o(L)+\\|\\ell\\|_*^2/2)\n \\label{eq:G-14}$$ (where the normalized excess limsup is at most zero).     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-quadratic-integration",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:04-quadratic\" label=\"lem:04-quadratic\"></span>Let $F$ be a bounded real symmetric operator on $\\mathcal X$, with $F\\ge gI$, $g>0$, and let $F-I$ be trace class. Let $l\\in\\mathcal X$ be real. Suppose that there are a smooth real vector test $b(s)$, odd under endpoint reflection, and a smooth real matrix kernel $\\mathscr C(s,v)$ satisfying $\\mathscr C(s,v)=\\mathscr C(v,s)^{\\mathsf t}$, odd under reflection in either variable, such that $$h_0(l,Q)=\\int b(s)^{\\mathsf t}Q(s)\\,ds,\\qquad\n h_0(Q,(F-I)Q)=\\iint Q(s)^{\\mathsf t}\\mathscr C(s,v)Q(v)\\,ds\\,dv.$$ Let $c\\in\\mathbb R$. For the compensated cost $$V(Q)=c+h_0(l,Q)+\\tfrac12h_0(Q,(F-I)Q),\\qquad\n m_F=c-\\tfrac12h_0(l,F^{-1}l),$$ one has $$\\log\\mathbb E_0e^{-V(Q)}\n \\le -m_F+CL+\\tfrac12\\sum_{\\lambda<1,\\ \\lambda\\in\\operatorname{spec}(F)}\n                    -\\log\\lambda .\n \\label{eq:G-18}$$ Here and below smooth compensated expressions are evaluated on empirical primitives by distributional pairing; their individual singular $h_0$-energies are not defined. For the sharp form used below, assume $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$, $g^{-1}+\\|F\\|+\\|l\\|_{\\mathcal X}\\le CL^a$ for fixed $C,a$, and polynomial physical derivative bounds for $b$ and $\\mathscr C$ in the convention above. Then for every fixed $D$, <a href=\"#eq:G-18\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-18\">[eq:G-18]</a> holds with $CL$ replaced by $o(L)$ and expectation restricted to $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D$. The error is uniform once these bounds and $D$ are fixed. The constant term $c$ is unrestricted: its factor $e^{-c}$ occurs on both sides of the estimate.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-quadratic-integration",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:04-quadratic\" label=\"lem:04-quadratic\"></span>Let $F$ be a bounded real symmetric operator on $\\mathcal X$, with $F\\ge gI$, $g>0$, and let $F-I$ be trace class. Let $l\\in\\mathcal X$ be real. Suppose that there are a smooth real vector test $b(s)$, odd under endpoint reflection, and a smooth real matrix kernel $\\mathscr C(s,v)$ satisfying $\\mathscr C(s,v)=\\mathscr C(v,s)^{\\mathsf t}$, odd under reflection in either variable, such that $$h_0(l,Q)=\\int b(s)^{\\mathsf t}Q(s)\\,ds,\\qquad\n h_0(Q,(F-I)Q)=\\iint Q(s)^{\\mathsf t}\\mathscr C(s,v)Q(v)\\,ds\\,dv.$$ Let $c\\in\\mathbb R$. For the compensated cost $$V(Q)=c+h_0(l,Q)+\\tfrac12h_0(Q,(F-I)Q),\\qquad\n m_F=c-\\tfrac12h_0(l,F^{-1}l),$$ one has $$\\log\\mathbb E_0e^{-V(Q)}\n \\le -m_F+CL+\\tfrac12\\sum_{\\lambda<1,\\ \\lambda\\in\\operatorname{spec}(F)}\n                    -\\log\\lambda .\n \\label{eq:G-18}$$ Here and below smooth compensated expressions are evaluated on empirical primitives by distributional pairing; their individual singular $h_0$-energies are not defined. For the sharp form used below, assume $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$, $g^{-1}+\\|F\\|+\\|l\\|_{\\mathcal X}\\le CL^a$ for fixed $C,a$, and polynomial physical derivative bounds for $b$ and $\\mathscr C$ in the convention above. Then for every fixed $D$, <a href=\"#eq:G-18\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-18\">[eq:G-18]</a> holds with $CL$ replaced by $o(L)$ and expectation restricted to $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D$. The error is uniform once these bounds and $D$ are fixed. The constant term $c$ is unrestricted: its factor $e^{-c}$ occurs on both sides of the estimate.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"

      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"lem:04-quadratic\" label=\"lem:04-quadratic\"></span>Let $F$ be a bounded real symmetric operator on $\\mathcal X$, with $F\\ge gI$, $g>0$, and let $F-I$ be trace class. Let $l\\in\\mathcal X$ be real. Suppose that there are a smooth real vector test $b(s)$, odd under endpoint reflection, and a smooth real matrix kernel $\\mathscr C(s,v)$ satisfying $\\mathscr C(s,v)=\\mathscr C(v,s)^{\\mathsf t}$, odd under reflection in either variable, such that $$h_0(l,Q)=\\int b(s)^{\\mathsf t}Q(s)\\,ds,\\qquad\n h_0(Q,(F-I)Q)=\\iint Q(s)^{\\mathsf t}\\mathscr C(s,v)Q(v)\\,ds\\,dv.$$ Let $c\\in\\mathbb R$. For the compensated cost $$V(Q)=c+h_0(l,Q)+\\tfrac12h_0(Q,(F-I)Q),\\qquad\n m_F=c-\\tfrac12h_0(l,F^{-1}l),$$ one has $$\\log\\mathbb E_0e^{-V(Q)}\n \\le -m_F+CL+\\tfrac12\\sum_{\\lambda<1,\\ \\lambda\\in\\operatorname{spec}(F)}\n                    -\\log\\lambda .\n \\label{eq:G-18}$$ Here and below smooth compensated expressions are evaluated on empirical primitives by distributional pairing; their individual singular $h_0$-energies are not defined. For the sharp form used below, assume $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$, $g^{-1}+\\|F\\|+\\|l\\|_{\\mathcal X}\\le CL^a$ for fixed $C,a$, and polynomial physical derivative bounds for $b$ and $\\mathscr C$ in the convention above. Then for every fixed $D$, <a href=\"#eq:G-18\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-18\">[eq:G-18]</a> holds with $CL$ replaced by $o(L)$ and expectation restricted to $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D$. The error is uniform once these bounds and $D$ are fixed. The constant term $c$ is unrestricted: its factor $e^{-c}$ occurs on both sides of the estimate.",
      "explanation": "Bounds quadratic perturbation expectations by the completed-square minimum and the negative logarithmic spectral cost, with a sharper restricted version.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-04-integer-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $|\\operatorname{Re}\\eta|\\le a_0<\\pi$ and $|\\operatorname{Im}\\eta|\\le b_0<\\infty$, with the parameter conventions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:03-trial-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:03-trial-bounds\">18</a>. For nonnegative integer dimensions $\\nu_\\sigma$, put $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\n =\\log|e^{i(\\eta+\\pi f+2\\pi j)M}F_\\nu(M)|,\n \\qquad v=\\operatorname{Im}M.$$ The following estimates hold.*\n\n1.  *If $M$ is real and $0\\le\\nu_\\sigma\\le C e^{\\gamma L}$, then, without any restriction on the explicit counts, $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le C_hL+C_hn_e-c_hd_e.$$ This bound is independent of the integer alias $j$.*\n\n\n2.  *Suppose $n_e$ is polynomially bounded, as in part 2 of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:03-trial-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:03-trial-bounds\">18</a>. In the same dimension range, for every real $v$ and integer $j$, $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le C_h L+C_h n_e-c_h d_e-2\\pi jv\n                     +C_h(v^2+(1+f)|v|).\n     \\label{eq:G-15}$$*\n\n\n\n3.  *Assume the common hypotheses of parts 3 and 4 of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:03-trial-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:03-trial-bounds\">18</a>, polynomial bounds on $n_e,|j|,|M|,\\ell_e$, and $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$. Choose the imaginary imbalance and trial density from part 3 of that proposition. Then $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le C_h L+C_h n_e-c_h d_e-c_\\delta U^2 L+o(L).\n     \\label{eq:G-16}$$ If $U\\le U_0$, the choice from part 4 instead gives $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le\\frac{3\\gamma L}{8}\n     -2L\\frac{\\operatorname{Re}(\\eta^2)}{4\\kappa}\n     +C\\varepsilon L+o(L)+C_h n_e-c_{h,\\varepsilon}d_e.\n     \\label{eq:G-17}$$ Here $\\delta$ can be taken sufficiently small without degrading the displayed damping and per-particle constants, and $U_0$ may then be fixed arbitrarily, as in part 4 of that proposition.*\n\n*The estimates are uniform once the parameters in the trial bounds and the polynomial size powers are fixed.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-particle-transport",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:04-contour\" label=\"lem:04-contour\"></span>Under the hypotheses of part 3 of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:04-integer-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:04-integer-bounds\">29</a>, choose the core projection $P$ of <a href=\"#eq:G-10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-10\">[eq:G-10]</a>, with $S=P\\mathcal X$, $R=S^\\perp$, and Schur complement $T_e$. For every prescribed fixed tail exponent, a sufficiently large fixed $D$ gives an exact contour deformation of the reference integral. On $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D$, its compensated cost is $C_0(Q+a(Q))-h_0(Q,Q)/2+o(1)$, where $$Q+a(Q)=iZ_0+Q_R+T_{SS}^{-1/2}Q_S\n             -T_{SS}^{-1}Pu_z-T_{SS}^{-1}T_{SR}Q_R.$$ The extra Jacobian, after the one-coordinate reference-density cancellation, has modulus $(1+o(1))|\\det T_{SS}|^{-1/2}$. The full cost $\\operatorname{Re}C_0(Q+a(Q))$ has Hessian $I$ on $S$ and $\\operatorname{Re}T_e$ on $R$, and its infimum is at least $m_z$. The complementary contribution is smaller than the prescribed stretched exponential.",
