      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-winding-data",
        "exact_quote": "The function $\\Lambda(\\chi;z)$ is rational in $e^{2iz}$, with denominator dividing $\\prod_i\\mathscr D(z-b_i)$, where $\\mathscr D$ is the local denominator in <a href=\"#eq:E-local-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:E-local-weights\">[eq:E-local-weights]</a>, and tends to $1+\\chi$ at both ends of this multiplicative coordinate. At the balanced specialization it is invariant under $z\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%\n  \\mapsto%\n  \\EndAccSupp{}%\n}3\\lambda-z$. For $u=b_i$ and generic remaining sites, $$\\Lambda(\\chi;u+j\\lambda)\\Lambda(\\chi;u)\n =\\begin{cases}\n   \\Lambda(\\chi;u+\\lambda),&j=2,\\\\\n   1,&j=3.\n  \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:05-annular-pinch}$$ Set $w=2i(z-z_0)$ and, with derivatives evaluated at $\\chi=0$, define $$\\begin{gathered}\n f(w)=\\partial_\\chi\\Lambda(\\chi;z),\\qquad\n h_2(w)=\\partial_\\chi^2\\Lambda(\\chi;z),\\qquad\n g(w)=h_2(w)-f(w)^2,\\\\\n (\\mathscr L v)(w)=v(w+i\\beta)+v(w-i\\beta)-v(w).\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:05-pressure-derivatives}$$ Then $\\mathscr L f$ and $\\mathscr L g$ vanish to orders $2m,m,m$ at $0,i\\beta,-i\\beta$, respectively. The functions $f-1$ and $h_2$ are even rational functions of $e^w$, vanish at both real infinities, and have poles only at the translates of these three knots by $i\\pi$, modulo $2\\pi i$, with the same respective order bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-winding-data",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-hardy-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $0<a<1$, the operators $D_a$ are bounded and satisfy $$\\operatorname{Re}D_a(k)\\ge c_a>0\n \\quad(k\\in\\mathbb R),\\qquad\n \\|(P_ND_a|_{P_NH})^{-1}\\|\\le c_a^{-1}.\n \\label{eq:05-coercivity}$$ Moreover, writing $\\|\\cdot\\|_{\\partial,p}$ for the sum of the two boundary $L^p$ norms, one has $$\\|e^{aw}(1-f)\\|_{L^p(\\operatorname{Im}w=y)}\n       \\le C_a e^{aA_N},\n \\qquad p=2,4,\\quad |y|\\le h+\\beta.\n \\label{eq:05-first-derivative-norms}$$ For fixed $0<a<1/2$, $$\\bigl|\\mathscr Lf(s)\\bigr|+\n \\bigl|\\mathscr Lg(s)\\bigr|\n \\le C_a|B_N(s)|\\bigl(1+e^{2a(A_N-s)}\\bigr),\n \\qquad s\\ge0.\n \\label{eq:05-jet-residual}$$ For $\\mathscr Lf$ alone, $2a$ can be replaced by any fixed $a\\in(0,1)$.",

      "exact_statement": "For every fixed $0<a<1$, the operators $D_a$ are bounded and satisfy $$\\operatorname{Re}D_a(k)\\ge c_a>0\n \\quad(k\\in\\mathbb R),\\qquad\n \\|(P_ND_a|_{P_NH})^{-1}\\|\\le c_a^{-1}.\n \\label{eq:05-coercivity}$$ Moreover, writing $\\|\\cdot\\|_{\\partial,p}$ for the sum of the two boundary $L^p$ norms, one has $$\\|e^{aw}(1-f)\\|_{L^p(\\operatorname{Im}w=y)}\n       \\le C_a e^{aA_N},\n \\qquad p=2,4,\\quad |y|\\le h+\\beta.\n \\label{eq:05-first-derivative-norms}$$ For fixed $0<a<1/2$, $$\\bigl|\\mathscr Lf(s)\\bigr|+\n \\bigl|\\mathscr Lg(s)\\bigr|\n \\le C_a|B_N(s)|\\bigl(1+e^{2a(A_N-s)}\\bigr),\n \\qquad s\\ge0.\n \\label{eq:05-jet-residual}$$ For $\\mathscr Lf$ alone, $2a$ can be replaced by any fixed $a\\in(0,1)$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-05-winding-suppression",
        "exact_quote": "On the balanced cylinder with period $V=iN\\sqrt3/2$, let $[\\ell]$ range over simple essential polygons modulo integer horizontal translations, and set $W(\\ell)=\\rho^{|\\ell|}$. If $d_x(\\ell)$ is their horizontal span, then $$\\sum_{[\\ell]}W(\\ell)\\asymp N^{-1},\\qquad\n \\sum_{[\\ell]}W(\\ell)d_x(\\ell)\\le C.\n \\label{eq:05-winding-moment}$$ The total weight of essential polygons separating the two marked points in the balanced two-marker geometry is bounded uniformly in $N$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-variable-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "With $\\widehat P=P_{x+\\lambda}$ for $j=2$, and the identity for $j=3$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n R_yR_{-y}=I,\\qquad P_{j\\lambda}=S_jS_j^{\\mathsf t},\\\\\n (P_x)_2(P_{x+j\\lambda})_1(I\\otimes S_j)\n   =d_j(x)(S_j\\otimes I)\\widehat P,\\qquad\n d_j(x)=1+nq_j\\frac{A_{x+j\\lambda}U_x}\n                          {e_{x+j\\lambda}e_x}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:05-variable-splitting}$$ The raw boxes also satisfy the three-box Yang–Baxter identity.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-variable-algebra",
          "exact_quote": "With $\\widehat P=P_{x+\\lambda}$ for $j=2$, and the identity for $j=3$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n R_yR_{-y}=I,\\qquad P_{j\\lambda}=S_jS_j^{\\mathsf t},\\\\\n (P_x)_2(P_{x+j\\lambda})_1(I\\otimes S_j)\n   =d_j(x)(S_j\\otimes I)\\widehat P,\\qquad\n d_j(x)=1+nq_j\\frac{A_{x+j\\lambda}U_x}\n                          {e_{x+j\\lambda}e_x}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:05-variable-splitting}$$ The raw boxes also satisfy the three-box Yang–Baxter identity.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "With $\\widehat P=P_{x+\\lambda}$ for $j=2$, and the identity for $j=3$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n R_yR_{-y}=I,\\qquad P_{j\\lambda}=S_jS_j^{\\mathsf t},\\\\\n (P_x)_2(P_{x+j\\lambda})_1(I\\otimes S_j)\n   =d_j(x)(S_j\\otimes I)\\widehat P,\\qquad\n d_j(x)=1+nq_j\\frac{A_{x+j\\lambda}U_x}\n                          {e_{x+j\\lambda}e_x}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:05-variable-splitting}$$ The raw boxes also satisfy the three-box Yang–Baxter identity.",
      "explanation": "Establishes local transfer identities with the scalar corrections produced by nonzero closed-loop weight.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-open-pinch",
        "exact_quote": "The matrices $t(z)$ commute and are invariant under $z\\leftrightarrow\\bar z$. At $\\lambda=\\lambda_0$, $x_i=\\lambda/2$, and $z=3\\lambda/2$, this is the transfer of two physical columns with vacant auxiliary boundaries. Its simple eigenvalue continuing $1$ will be denoted by $\\Lambda_o(n;z)$. It is rational and $\\pi$-periodic in $z$, equals one at $n=0$, and tends to $1+n$ at both imaginary infinities. For $u=x_i$, $v=u+j\\lambda$, its pinch rule is $$\\Lambda_o(n;v)\\Lambda_o(n;u)\n =c_bc_t\\prod_kd_j(u-x_k)d_j(\\bar v-x_k)\n \\begin{cases}\n \\Lambda_o(n;u+\\lambda),&j=2,\\\\\n 1,&j=3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:05-open-pinch}$$ where $w_0=u+v-3\\lambda$ and $$c_b=\\frac{e_{w_0}+nq_j\\mathfrak m(\\bar u)A_{w_0}}{L_{w_0}},\n \\qquad\n c_t=\\frac{e_{-w_0}+nq_j\\mathfrak m(v)A_{-w_0}}{L_{-w_0}}.\n \\label{eq:05-boundary-scalars}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-open-pinch",
          "exact_quote": "The matrices $t(z)$ commute and are invariant under $z\\leftrightarrow\\bar z$. At $\\lambda=\\lambda_0$, $x_i=\\lambda/2$, and $z=3\\lambda/2$, this is the transfer of two physical columns with vacant auxiliary boundaries. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Honeycomb polygon** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A polygon is an unoriented simple cycle of the dual graph.
- **Critical polygon weight** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Its length $\|P\|$ is its number of vertices, and its critical weight is $\rho^{\|P\|}$, where $$\rho=\frac1{\sqrt{2+\sqrt2}}.$$
- **Balanced cylinder geometry** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For even $N=2m$, quotient by the translation $iN\sqrt3/2$ and mark the images of $0$ and $m e^{2\pi i/3}$.
- **Marker separation by a polygon** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A polygon *separates the markers* if they lie in distinct components of its complement on that sphere.
- **Loop fugacity and cylinder partition** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Give every separating polygon an additional factor $\chi$, called its loop fugacity, and define $$Z_N(\chi)=\sum_{\mathcal P}\prod_{P\in\mathcal P} \chi\rho^{\|P\|}.$$
- **Vacuum state** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A vacuum state records the pairings of occupied ports made by paths on one side of a cut, with no external source.
- **Port** ([source](#sec:02-cylinder)): A port is the midpoint of a tile edge.
- **Critical coefficient mass** ([source](#eq:06-positive-coefficients)): For each integer $a\ge0$, let $w_N(a)$ be the critical mass of families of exactly $a$ pairwise vertex-disjoint polygons, each separating the two markers.
- **Explicit particle configuration** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Keep as explicit particles $\zeta$ all main ones outside the wall and all far particles everywhere, regarded as positive counting measures by label (unordered, with the factorial measures of the sector formula).
- **Compensated empirical observable** ([source](#pm-h-22)): The smooth (near-diagonal compensated) observable uses $$\Phi_M(q)=\mathcal E(m'p_L+q,\zeta)-\tfrac12(q,H_{\rm ref}q).$$
- **Planar nesting partition** ([source](#pm-h-68)): $$ A simple contractible cylinder polygon lifts to translates of a simple planar polygon with disjoint interior disks. The boundary lifts are disjoint; if two of their bounded disks overlapped, planar separation would force nesting, which is impossible for distinct translates of equal-area bounded disks. Thus its disk projects injectively to the cylinder. For a fixed planar translation class, the number of placements modulo the period containing the marked point is again at most $CN(1+R)$: there are $N$ vertical translation classes modulo period and its horizontal range must straddle the marker. Counting all these placements, including those not valid under projection, is an upper bound. The same dyadic estimate applies. For a fixed remaining nest, sum the removed polygons while ignoring disjointness both among themselves and with the nest. If their total single-polygon weight is at most $C_{c_0}$, this contributes at most $e^{\chi C_{c_0}}$. Positivity gives the reverse inequality by taking the empty removed set. ◻ </details> Let $P_\chi(r)$ be the planar partition over finite disjoint nests around one face center, with every polygon of diameter at most $r$, each weighted by $\chi\rho^{\|P\|}$; include the empty nest.
- **Middle-face strip nesting mass** ([source](#pm-h-69)): Let $S_k$ be the partition sum of nests around a face center $f$ on the middle line of the strip $\mathcal S_{2k}$ of $2k$ triangular bands.
- **Raw first-length mass** ([source](#pm-h-71)): Write $$\mathcal M_N(a)=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal P_N(a)} \rho^{n(\gamma)}n(\gamma),$$ where $n(\gamma)$ counts visited honeycomb vertices.

| q-critical-vertex-activity | Critical vertex activity | = | $\frac1{\sqrt{2+\sqrt2}}$ | Critical polygon and path weights. |

| q-two-site-contraction | Two-site contraction | = | $1+\chi(2c)^2\frac{xy}{(x+y)^2}$ | Paired generic scaling across the groups. |
| q-logarithmic-wall | Logarithmic wall scale | = | $\frac{\log N}{\gamma}$ | Growing even sizes. |
| q-balanced-denominator-logarithm | Logarithm of the balanced normalized denominator modulus | = | $\frac5{24}\log N+o(\log N)$ | Even sizes tending to infinity. |
| q-explicit-count-energy | Squared real imbalance plus squared explicit variable count | ≤ | $C_h L(B(x)+1)$ | The smoothing parameter remains fixed. |
| q-explicit-excursion-energy | Total exterior excursion | ≤ | $C(B(x)+1)$ | The wall and positive energy use the stated centering conventions. |
| q-relative-trace-norm | Trace norm of the relative perturbation | ≤ | $CL$ | Growing wall scale. |
| q-relative-hermitian-gap | Hermitian part of the relative operator | ≥ | $cL^{-2}I$ | The same gap is asserted for the indicated compressions and singular values. |
| q-small-eigenvalue-count | Small-eigenvalue counting function | ≤ | $CL\sqrt t$ | Thresholds in the stated small positive interval. |
| q-relative-determinant-cost | Negative half logarithm of the relative determinant modulus | = | $(7\gamma/8)L+o(L)$ | Growing wall scale. |
| q-core-trace-error | Trace norm error of the smooth core approximation | ≤ | $\xi L$ | Fix the tolerance before the large-size limit. |
| q-unbounded-fugacity-majorant | Cylinder partition function at hyperbolic fugacity | ≤ | $\exp\!\bigl(C\log N\,(1+v^2)\bigr)$ | Sufficiently large even sizes; constants are independent of the fugacity parameter. |
| q-count-coefficient-tail | Unnormalized separating-family coefficient mass | ≤ | $\exp\!\left(C\log N-\frac{ca^2}{\log N}\right)$ | Sufficiently large even sizes and all nonnegative integer counts. |
| q-open-strip-pressure-expansion | Open-strip polygon pressure | = | $c_1N+c_2+O(N^{-1})$ | Growing strip width with vertical placement multiplicity. |
| q-planar-diameter-tail | Critical weight of polygon shapes above the diameter cutoff | ≤ | $\frac{C}{(1+R)^2}$ | All nonnegative diameter cutoffs. |
| q-planar-nesting-scaling | Planar nesting partition function | = | $r^{\tau(\chi)/2+o(1)}$ | Radius tends to infinity at fixed positive fugacity. |
| q-middle-strip-nesting-scaling | Middle-face strip nesting mass | = | $k^{1/12+o(1)}$ | Strip height tends to infinity. |
| q-raw-first-length-majorant | Unnormalized strip first-length mass | ≤ | $N^{13/12+o(1)}$ | Growing height; the source is fixed. |
| q-conditioned-path-length | Expected visited-vertex count conditional on macroscopic penetration | = | $N^{4/3+o(1)}$ | Growing strip height under the specified conditioning event. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-critical-walk-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-02-critical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-02-critical): Weak bridge decomposition and contradiction with polynomial strip-crossing mass. -> Critical activity identification and subexponential walk bound.
- **step-import-open-polynomial** [claim](#stmt-prop-e-open-input) / [proof](#pm-h-13): Use the explicitly imported open-strip polynomial identities and interpolation data. -> Finite polynomial input for the periodic construction.
- **step-homogeneous-vacuum** [claim](#stmt-lem-02-vacuum-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-02-vacuum-polynomial): Take a homogeneous limit of the open interpolation and prove covariance by polynomial uniqueness. -> Homogeneous periodic vacuum and normalized quotient.
- **step-transfer-stationarity** [claim](#stmt-lem-02-algebraic-stationarity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-02-algebraic-stationarity): Interpolate the transfer residual at identity-box and reflected nodes. -> Algebraic stationarity of the vacuum.
- **step-periodic-slab-decay** [claim](#stmt-lem-02-periodic-slab) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-02-periodic-slab): Combine rational generating functions with recoverable projection-invalid connectors. -> Exponential spanning-path decay at fixed circumference.
- **step-physical-vacuum** [claim](#stmt-prop-02-cylinder-vacuum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-02-cylinder-vacuum): Use spanning decay to bound the nonempty transfer block and identify its normalized fixed state. -> Absolutely convergent physical half-cylinder vacuum.
- **step-characterize-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-02-characterization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-02-characterization): Derive reductions and paired limits, then use polynomial root counts for uniqueness. -> Unique rational characterization of the cylinder observable.
- **step-finite-label-formula** [claim](#stmt-e-finite-cylinder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-finite-cylinder): Cancel label-sum poles and verify every contraction characterization property. -> Exact finite label formula.
- **step-residue-sectors** [claim](#stmt-e-contour-sectors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-contour-sectors): Move particle contours, cancel unbound signed species coefficientwise, and identify surviving site strings. -> Absolutely convergent contour-sector representation and confluent continuation.
- **step-sector-signs** [claim](#stmt-lem-02-sector-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-02-sector-signs): Combine fused log-factor exponents and count the remaining sign parity. -> Sign-normalized kernels and exact imbalance phase.
- **step-balanced-normalization** [claim](#stmt-e-balanced-denominator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-balanced-denominator): Represent the Pfaffian ratio as an accretive multiplier compression and evaluate its trace-density limit. -> Nonzero denominator and its logarithmic asymptotic.
- **step-exact-centering** [claim](#stmt-e-centering) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-centering): Match Fourier symbols and tails to identify the mean convolution and extracted site product. -> Exact compensated mean and scalar centering factor.
- **step-real-coercivity** [claim](#stmt-e-coercivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-coercivity): Check positivity of the species Fourier matrix across low, compact, and large frequencies. -> Two-sided energy norm comparison.
- **step-compensated-sector** [claim](#stmt-lem-03-compensated-sector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-03-compensated-sector): Restore diagonal compensation, retain reference masses, and track extracted signs and regularization factors. -> Smooth compensated physical sector identity.
- **step-smoothing-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-03-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-03-smoothing): Compare regularized kernels in Fourier space and control explicit cell counts by duality. -> Uniform smoothing inequality.
- **step-explicit-count-control** [claim](#stmt-e-explicit-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-explicit-counts): Test cumulative exterior mass and group imbalance against controlled Sobolev probes. -> Count, imbalance, and excursion damping.
- **step-imaginary-trials** [claim](#stmt-prop-03-trial-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-03-trial-bounds): Choose progressively sharper imaginary trial fields and complete the low-frequency real-field squares. -> Real, alias, and sharp quadratic tilt action bounds.
- **step-smooth-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-03-kernel-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-03-kernel-regularity): Use folded endpoint coordinates and divided differences to cancel diagonal logarithms. -> Smooth reflected compensated kernel with derivative and decay bounds.
- **step-smooth-source** [claim](#stmt-lem-03-source-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-03-source-regularity): Separate the centered mean into a polynomial coefficient term and exponentially small endpoint corrections. -> Polynomially controlled source and constant terms.
- **step-relative-spectrum** [claim](#stmt-e-spectral-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-spectral-bounds): Sum cellwise kernel coefficients for trace bounds and use local difference energies with min–max for the spectral edge. -> Trace bound, gap, and small-value count.
- **step-relative-determinant** [claim](#stmt-e-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-determinant): Compute limiting trace words, clip the logarithm near zero, and integrate the limiting Fourier determinant. -> Leading relative determinant cost.
- **step-smooth-core** [claim](#stmt-e-finite-core) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-finite-core): Truncate cell frequencies, smooth retained modes, remove endpoint cells, and orthogonalize the induced fields. -> Smooth interior finite core with trace tolerance.
- **step-schur-control** [claim](#stmt-e-schur-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-schur-comparison): Transfer Hermitian positivity through the Schur graph and control clipped logarithms by trace error and small-value counts. -> Core determinant comparison and negligible residual logarithmic cost in the iterated limit.
- **step-circle-source-comparison** [claim](#stmt-e-circle-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-circle-comparison): Split the circle Gram determinant into sine and cosine blocks and bound the holomorphic exponential using Schur orthogonality. -> Finite circle comparison inequality.
- **step-coarse-source** [claim](#stmt-e-reference-source) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-reference-source): Apply Cauchy–Schwarz to the geometric mean of the Jacobi weights and restore normalization masses. -> Coarse source bound at every allowed integer dimension.
- **step-sharp-source** [claim](#stmt-e-sharp-reference-source) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-sharp-reference-source): Transport the particles oppositely for the Jacobi weights and retain the normalization gain on the controlled event. -> Source bound with vanishing normalized volume loss.
- **step-quadratic-reference-integration** [claim](#stmt-e-quadratic-integration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-quadratic-integration): Complete the square, linearize the negative perturbation by a Gaussian field, and pass through smooth finite truncations. -> Coarse and sharp compensated quadratic integration bounds.
- **step-particle-transport** [claim](#stmt-e-particle-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-particle-transport): Realize an affine core transformation by configuration-dependent particle displacements, use Stokes’ theorem, and evaluate the reduced Jacobian. -> Exact contour deformation with determinant gain and negligible complementary contribution.
- **step-integer-sector-estimates** [claim](#stmt-prop-04-integer-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-particle-transport-2): Apply real quadratic integration for coarse estimates and the transported core determinant for sharp estimates. -> Uniform bounds for integer-dimensional electric sectors.
- **step-hermite-approximation** [claim](#stmt-lem-04-hermite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-04-hermite): Estimate the weighted Hermite coefficient tail using Gaussian generating functions and Mehler’s formula. -> Weighted polynomial approximation with controlled holomorphic coefficients.
- **step-dimension-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-prop-04-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-04-hermite-2): Combine polynomial approximation, endpoint-subset inversion, and continued near-index factorial moments. -> Holomorphic dimension approximation with arbitrary prescribed integer-grid precision.
- **step-grid-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-04-grid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-04-grid): Approximate on a large dimension polydisk and use Chebyshev derivative bounds to transfer integer-grid control. -> Sharp bound at the complex prescribed dimensions.
- **step-sector-summation** [claim](#stmt-prop-04-sector-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-04-sector-sum): Truncate damped sectors, replace by holomorphic approximants, expand the rectangle kernel into aliases, and shift each retained alias. -> Locally uniform complex bound for the normalized contour numerator.
- **step-cylinder-exponent** [claim](#stmt-e-cylinder-exponent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-04-sector-sum-2): Restore the scalar normalization, propagate near equality from the empty-configuration value by superharmonicity, and use coefficient positivity. -> Matching exponent at every fixed positive fugacity.
- **step-uniform-fugacity** [claim](#stmt-e-all-fugacity-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-all-fugacity-bound): Retain untruncated real sectors, sum explicit factorial measures, and complete the imbalance square. -> Uniform majorant over unbounded hyperbolic fugacity.
- **step-count-tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-11-polygon-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-11-polygon-tail): Use coefficient positivity and optimize the uniform fugacity majorant. -> Gaussian-type count coefficient tail.
- **step-annular-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-05-winding-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-05-winding-data): Fuse annular auxiliaries and differentiate the pinch relations before confluence. -> Rational pressure data and prescribed jets.
- **step-hardy-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-05-hardy-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-05-hardy-interpolation): Realize rational partial fractions as a coercive compressed multiplier and divide residuals by the prescribed inner function. -> Uniform interpolation inverse and boundary and residual bounds.
- **step-winding-pressure** [claim](#stmt-lem-05-pressure-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-05-pressure-bounds): Invert the shift operator, translate to the edge limit, and prove the limiting moment positive by one-sided factorization. -> First and second winding-pressure estimates.
- **step-essential-removal** [claim](#stmt-prop-05-winding-suppression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-05-winding-suppression): Interpret pressure derivatives as polygon cluster sums and bound horizontal placements through intersection counts. -> Essential mass and span bounds and bounded deletion cost.
- **step-variable-local-algebra** [claim](#stmt-lem-05-variable-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-05-variable-algebra): Check occupancy-sector coefficients and use interpolation to prove the raw three-box identity. -> Local identities with nonzero loop-weight corrections.
- **step-open-transfer-pinches** [claim](#stmt-lem-05-open-pinch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-05-open-pinch): Exchange auxiliary lines through boundary tensors and fuse descending pairs with the explicit boundary scalars. -> Commuting reflected open transfer and exact eigenvalue pinches.
- **step-open-forced-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-05-open-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-05-open-interpolation): Compare the enlarged unknown and equation jet spaces, remove bulk and boundary forcing, and estimate the residual. -> Open-pressure center evaluation with inverse-width error.
- **step-open-polygon-pressure** [claim](#stmt-prop-05-open-pressure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-05-open-pressure): Differentiate at zero loop weight and count horizontal density and vertical placements of polygon shapes. -> Linear polygon pressure expansion with constant correction.
- **step-planar-diameter-tail** [claim](#stmt-e-polygon-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-polygon-tail): Use monotone convergence and differences of two strip widths, then rotate among lattice band directions. -> Quadratic planar diameter tail.
- **step-macroscopic-removal** [claim](#stmt-lem-05-macroscopic-removal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-05-macroscopic-removal): Count enclosing placements by diameter scales and sum the polygon tail before relaxing compatibility. -> Bounded aggregate large-polygon weight and deletion cost.
- **step-planar-nest-comparison** [claim](#stmt-e-planar-nesting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-planar-nesting): Delete essential and large contractible polygons, identify independent embedded planar neighborhoods, and take logarithms. -> Bounded-factor squared planar comparison and planar nesting exponent.
- **step-middle-strip-nests** [claim](#stmt-e-strip-nesting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-strip-nesting): Compare middle-face nests with diameter-restricted planar nests using bounded macroscopic removal. -> Middle-strip nesting exponent.
- **step-length-nest-identity** [claim](#stmt-e-length-nest) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-length-nest): Cut an interior edge, classify winding collisions, iterate the enclosing-polygon recurrence, and exhaust the strip. -> Positive comparison between edge visits and adjacent-face nest masses.
- **step-raw-first-moment** [claim](#stmt-e-raw-first-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-raw-first-moment): Sum internal edge visits over horizontal translation orbits and bound each adjacent-face nest by a centered strip nest. -> Unnormalized first-length upper bound.
- **step-conditioned-length** [claim](#stmt-prop-02-length-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-02-length-reduction): Identify the penetration mass, bound the restricted visit mass from central edge orbits, and divide by the conditioning mass. -> Conditional expected length exponent.

          "anchor": "stmt-e-balanced-denominator",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:02-denominator\" label=\"thm:02-denominator\"></span>For $N=2m\\ge2$, with exactly $m$ sites at each of $0,-i\\beta$, the normalization $h_N$ is nonzero. As $N\\to\\infty$ through these even sizes, $$\\label{eq:02-denominator}\n \\log\\left|\n \\frac{\\widetilde h_N^2}\n      {(\\gamma/2)^N\\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2}\n \\right|\n =\\frac5{24}\\log N+o(\\log N).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-balanced-normalization",
      "inputs": [
        "Vacuum Pfaffian",
        "Balanced confluent derivative evaluations"
      ],
      "method": "Represent the Pfaffian ratio as an accretive multiplier compression and evaluate its trace-density limit.",
      "output": "Nonzero denominator and its logarithmic asymptotic.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-balanced-denominator"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-centering",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-centering",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-centering\" label=\"lem:03-centering\"></span>The particle–site kernel satisfies $V=H_{\\cdot n}*\\varrho$. Combining the site–site factors with $\\exp((\\mu,H\\mu)/2)$ gives $$C_*^N\\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2,\\qquad\n C_*=\\frac{H_*(0)}{\\sqrt{B_s(0)}}\n     =\\frac{(1+d)\\gamma}{4d\\cos(\\beta/2)},$$ with the fixed signs prescribed by the contour-sector formula. Here the logarithmic site coordinates $a_i$ are the balanced confluent sites: $m$ copies of $0$ and $m$ copies of $-i\\beta$. The function $H_*$ is the removable analytic function used in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:02-denominator\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:02-denominator\">[thm:02-denominator]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-centering"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-coercivity",

          "anchor": "stmt-e-coercivity",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-coercivity\" label=\"lem:03-coercivity\"></span>Suppose that the components of $x$ are initially smooth rapidly decreasing real densities and that $\\int d_x=0$. The tail $-w_Iw_J|u|$ contributes exactly $\\|D\\|_2^2$; residual real kernels are integrable. We have $$B(x)\\asymp\\|D\\|_2^2+\\sum_I\\|x_I\\|_{-1/2}^2,\\qquad\n \\|x_I\\|_{-1/2}^2=\\int(1+k^2)^{-1/2}|\\widehat x_I|^2\\,dk/(2\\pi).$$ The form extends accordingly and controls $\\|D\\|_{1/2}^2=\\int (1+k^2)^{1/2}|\\widehat D|^2\\,dk/(2\\pi)$ also.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }

      "output": "Two-sided energy norm comparison.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-coercivity"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-03-compensated-sector",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-exact-centering",
        "step-sector-signs"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-03-compensated-sector",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an explicit configuration $\\zeta$ with generic distinct real coordinates and a physical integer imbalance $M$ for which $m'_\\sigma\\ge0$. After division of the right-hand side of <a href=\"#eq:02-contour-sectors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:02-contour-sectors\">[eq:02-contour-sectors]</a> by $(\\gamma/2)^N\\prod_{i<j}H_*(a_i-a_j)^2$, its contribution at $M,\\zeta$ is $$c_\\zeta\\,e^{i(\\eta+\\pi f)M}\n \\int A(X_{m'},\\zeta)\\exp\\{-\\Phi_M(X_{m'}-m'p_L)\\},\n \\qquad A(X,\\zeta)=\\exp\\{-(X,R_h\\zeta)\\}.$$ The integral includes the two *unnormalized* reference measures. The explicit integration measures and their species factorials are retained outside it. The prefactor $c_\\zeta$ is independent of $M$, satisfies $|c_\\zeta|\\le\\exp(C_hn_e)$, and uses the fixed sign convention of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:02-sector-signs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:02-sector-signs\">11</a>. Moreover $0\\le A\\le1$. Sectors with a negative $m'_\\sigma$ contribute zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-compensated-sector",
      "inputs": [
        "Exact centering",
        "Explicit and interior particle split"
      ],
      "method": "Restore diagonal compensation, retain reference masses, and track extracted signs and regularization factors.",
      "output": "Smooth compensated physical sector identity.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-03-compensated-sector"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-03-smoothing",

        "step-real-coercivity"
      ],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-03-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "For the trial energy defined above, set $$x=\\operatorname{Re}\\alpha-\\mu+\\psi*\\zeta,\\quad z=\\operatorname{Im}\\alpha$$ with all species included for $x$, only near entries nonzero for $z$. These are weighted-total neutral. Then $$\\operatorname{Re}\\mathcal E(\\alpha,\\zeta)\n \\ \\ge\\ B(x)-B(z)-(x,\\operatorname{Im}H\\,z)\n -s(h)(B(x)+B(z)+1)-C_h n_e,\\qquad s(h)\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=27F6}%\n  \\longrightarrow%\n  \\EndAccSupp{}%\n}0.\n \\label{eq:G-1}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-explicit-counts",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"cor:03-counts\" label=\"cor:03-counts\"></span>Constants may depend on the fixed smoothing parameter $h$. One has $$|\\operatorname{Re}M|^2+n_e^2\\le C_h L(B(x)+1),\\qquad\n \\ell_e:=\\sum_{v\\in\\zeta} (|v|-L)_+\\le C(B(x)+1).\n \\label{eq:G-3}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-explicit-count-control",
      "inputs": [
        "Smoothing cell-count bound",
        "Coercive cumulative mass"

      "method": "Test cumulative exterior mass and group imbalance against controlled Sobolev probes.",
      "output": "Count, imbalance, and excursion damping.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-explicit-counts"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-03-trial-bounds",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-smoothing-control",
        "step-explicit-count-control"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-03-trial-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $a_0<\\pi$ and $b_0<\\infty$, and let $\\eta=a+ib_1$ satisfy $|a|\\le a_0$, $|b_1|\\le b_0$. The following bounds hold uniformly for admissible real parts of $\\alpha$, with the imaginary fields specified below. In parts 1–3, $h>0$ is fixed and sufficiently small.*\n\n1.  *If $M$ is real and $z=0$, then for sufficiently small fixed $h>0$, with no truncation of the explicit counts, $$\\mathcal A\\ge cB(x)-C_hL-C_hn_e.$$*\n\n2.  *Suppose $n_e$ is polynomially bounded, and that $p_L$ has the properties stated above. For any $v=\\operatorname{Im}M\\in\\mathbb R$ and any $j\\in\\mathbb Z$, the choice $z_\\sigma=-\\sigma vp_L$ gives $$\\mathcal A\\ge cB(x)-C_hL-C_hn_e\n           +2\\pi jv-C_h\\bigl(v^2+(1+f)|v|\\bigr).$$ There is no polynomial restriction on $j$ in this estimate.*\n\n3.  *Suppose in addition that $|j|$ is polynomially bounded. There are a value $v=\\operatorname{Im}M$, independent of $\\operatorname{Re}M$, and an imaginary field $z$ with the required totals such that $$\\mathcal A\\ge cB(x)+c_\\delta U^2L-C_hL-C_hn_e.$$ The value $v$ is polynomially bounded. Each $z_\\sigma+\\sigma vp_L$ is the derivative of a smooth primitive supported in $|u|\\le(1-\\delta/2)L$, with polynomial bounds on all required fixed-order derivatives. Moreover, the number of far particles in $[-(1-2\\delta)L,(1-2\\delta)L]$ is at most $CU$ for large $L$.*\n\n4.  *Under the same polynomial hypotheses, suppose $U\\le U_0$. Choose a desired error $\\varepsilon>0$, then $h$ sufficiently small, then $\\delta$ sufficiently small. The constant $U_0$ may be fixed arbitrarily after these choices. There are a value $v=\\operatorname{Im}M$, independent of $\\operatorname{Re}M$, and an imaginary field $z$, with the same support and polynomial regularity properties as in part 3, such that $$\\mathcal A\\ge c'_\\varepsilon B(x)\n           +2L\\frac{\\operatorname{Re}(\\eta^2)}{4\\kappa}\n           -C\\varepsilon L-o(L)-C_hn_e.$$ The selected $v$ is polynomially bounded. The retained coefficient $c'_\\varepsilon>0$ can be taken independent of $\\delta,U_0$. On these bounded-$U$ configurations, integrating the explicit factorial measures with the factor $\\exp(C_hn_e)$, while using a fixed portion of the retained $B(x)$-damping, costs at most $$\\exp\\bigl(C_{h,\\varepsilon}\\delta L+o(L)\\bigr).$$ The same conclusion allows further fixed exponential factors per explicit particle after reducing $\\delta$ as needed.*\n\n*The constants are uniform once the polynomial bounds and the previously fixed parameters are fixed. In part 4 the asymptotic limit is taken only after $\\varepsilon,h,\\delta,U_0$ have been chosen in that order; constants depending on those fixed choices are allowed in $o(L)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],

        "Phase-tail decomposition"

      "output": "Real, alias, and sharp quadratic tilt action bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-03-trial-bounds"
    },

          "exact_quote": "The kernel $K$ is smooth, extending evenly by reflection in each endpoint variable. $K$ and all derivatives of total order $l$ satisfy bounds $C^{l+1}(l!)^P(1+L)$, and $\\mathscr K$ and its derivatives satisfy $C^{l+1}(l!)^P e^{-c|s-v|}$. For the latter estimates one only extends locally across endpoints. Here and below $\\operatorname{poly}(L)$ prefactors may replace $1+L$ without harm.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-03-kernel-regularity"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-03-source-regularity",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-03-source-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $|M|,n_e,\\ell_e$ have fixed polynomial bounds in $L$. In the $s$-coordinate, the linear source $$b=H_{nn}(m'p_L-\\mu)+\\widetilde H_{ne}\\zeta$$ in $\\Phi_M$ is smooth and even under endpoint reflection. Its derivatives of order $r$ are bounded by $\\operatorname{poly}(L)C^{r+1}(r!)^P$, for a fixed Gevrey order $P$, and it has the core analytic continuation just described. Writing $$E_e=\\tfrac12(\\zeta,H\\zeta)_{\\rm off},$$ the difference $\\mathcal E(m'p_L,\\zeta)-E_e$ is polynomially bounded in absolute value. The unchanged explicit-pair term $E_e$, independent of $M$, has real part bounded below by a negative polynomial; no upper bound is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],

    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-spectral-bounds",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-smooth-kernel",
        "step-real-coercivity"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-spectral-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-spectral\" label=\"lem:03-spectral\"></span>On the complexification of $\\mathcal X$, let $$T=I+j^*\\mathscr K j,\n \\qquad A_T=\\tfrac12(T+T^*).$$ There are constants independent of $L$ such that $\\|T-I\\|_1\\le CL$, $\\|T\\|\\le C$, and $$A_T\\ge cL^{-2}I,\n \\qquad N_{A_T}(t):=\\dim\\mathbf1_{(-\\infty,t)}(A_T)\n       \\le CL\\sqrt t\\quad(0<t<t_0).\n \\label{eq:G-8}$$ The same lower gap and counting estimate hold for the singular values of $T$, and for every orthogonal compression of $T$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],

      "inputs": [
        "Smooth mixed kernel",
        "Coercivity",
        "Reference Hilbert embedding"
      ],
      "method": "Sum cellwise kernel coefficients for trace bounds and use local difference energies with min–max for the spectral edge.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-spectral-bounds"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-determinant",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-e-determinant",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:03-determinant\" label=\"thm:03-determinant\"></span>The Fredholm determinant satisfies $$-\\tfrac12\\log|\\det T|=(7\\gamma/8)L+o(L).\n \\label{eq:G-9}$$",

        }

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-finite-core",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-relative-spectrum"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-finite-core",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-core\" label=\"lem:03-core\"></span>For every sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$ and all sufficiently large $L$, there are $\\delta_*>0$, independent of $L$, and a real orthogonal projection $P$ in $\\mathcal X$, of rank $O_\\xi(L)$, whose range consists of smooth fields supported in $|s|\\le(1-\\delta_*)L$, such that $$\\|T-I-P(T-I)P\\|_1\\le\\xi L.\n \\label{eq:G-10}$$ An orthonormal basis of its range can be chosen with every required fixed derivative bounded by a polynomial in $L$. The images of these basis fields under $h_0$ and $H_{\\rm form}$, regarded as test densities for the $ds$ pairing, have the same bounds and smooth odd reflections at the endpoints. Constants may depend on $\\xi$ and on the derivative order.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-smooth-core",
      "inputs": [
        "Trace-class kernel expansion",
        "Reference inverse kernel"
      ],
      "method": "Truncate cell frequencies, smooth retained modes, remove endpoint cells, and orthogonalize the induced fields.",
      "output": "Smooth interior finite core with trace tolerance.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-finite-core"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-schur-comparison",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-smooth-core",
        "step-relative-spectrum"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-schur-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-schur\" label=\"lem:03-schur\"></span>Let $S=\\operatorname{ran}P$, $R=S^\\perp$, and $$T_e=T_{RR}-T_{RS}T_{SS}^{-1}T_{SR}.$$ The Hermitian part $\\operatorname{Re}T_e=(T_e+T_e^*)/2$ has the gap and small-eigenvalue count in <a href=\"#eq:G-8\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-8\">[eq:G-8]</a>. Moreover, $$\\left|\\log|\\det T_{SS}|-\\log|\\det T|\\right|=o_\\xi(L),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\lambda<1\\\\\n        \\lambda\\in\\operatorname{spec}(\\operatorname{Re}T_e)}}\n       -\\log\\lambda=o_\\xi(L).\n \\label{eq:G-11}$$ Eigenvalues are counted with multiplicity. Here the notation means $$\\lim_{\\xi\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{L\\to\\infty}\n       \\frac{|o_\\xi(L)|}{L}=0.$$ It does not assert an $o(L)$ error for a fixed $\\xi$. One may take errors bounded respectively by $C\\xi^{1/5}L$ and $C\\xi^{1/3}(1+|\\log\\xi|)L$, after the core approximation is made with tolerance $\\xi$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-schur-control",

        "Spectral gap and count"

      "method": "Transfer Hermitian positivity through the Schur graph and control clipped logarithms by trace error and small-value counts.",
      "output": "Core determinant comparison and negligible residual logarithmic cost in the iterated limit.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-schur-comparison"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-circle-comparison",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-circle-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:03-circle\" label=\"lem:03-circle\"></span>For a real smooth test $f(t)=f_0+2\\sum_{k\\ge1} f_k\\cos kt$, with smooth even reflection, put $w_\\pm=2(1\\pm\\cos t)$. Then $$J_n(w_+ e^f)J_n(w_- e^f)e^{-2n f_0}\\le \\exp\\sum_{k\\ge1} k |f_k|^2 .\n \\label{eq:G-12}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-circle-source-comparison",
      "inputs": [

        "Smooth cosine test"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-reference-source",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-circle-source-comparison"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-reference-source",
          "exact_quote": "For two independent normalized reference ensembles with dimensions $0\\le\\nu_\\sigma\\le C_0e^{\\gamma L}$, $L\\ge1$, and every real smooth reflected test $f_\\sigma$, one has $$\\label{eq:G-13}\n \\mathbb E_0 e^{\\ell(Q)}\n \\le \\prod_\\sigma J_{\\nu_\\sigma}(w_0)^{-1}\n       \\exp\\bigl(\\|\\ell\\|_*^2/2\\bigr)\n \\le\\exp\\bigl(C L+\\|\\ell\\|_*^2/2\\bigr).$$ The constant depends only on $C_0$ and $\\gamma$; a zero-dimensional ensemble consists of the empty configuration and has partition mass one.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-coarse-source",
      "inputs": [
        "Circle comparison",
        "Legendre partition normalization"
      ],
      "method": "Apply Cauchy–Schwarz to the geometric mean of the Jacobi weights and restore normalization masses.",
      "output": "Coarse source bound at every allowed integer dimension.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-reference-source"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-sharp-reference-source",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-coarse-source"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-sharp-reference-source",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:03-uniform-source\" label=\"thm:03-uniform-source\"></span>If $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$, $\\mathcal G=\\{\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D\\}$ for fixed $D$, and the tests arise from real smooth even-reflected $f_\\sigma(s)$ with polynomial derivative bounds in the $s$-coordinate as specified above, then uniformly, $$\\mathbb E_0[\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal G} e^{\\ell(Q)}]\n \\le \\exp(o(L)+\\|\\ell\\|_*^2/2)\n \\label{eq:G-14}$$ (where the normalized excess limsup is at most zero). The error is uniform once $D$, the polynomial derivative bounds, and the constants implicit in $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$ are fixed.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-sharp-source",
      "inputs": [
        "Circle comparison",
        "Comparable reference dimensions",
        "Polynomial physical tests"
      ],
      "method": "Transport the particles oppositely for the Jacobi weights and retain the normalization gain on the controlled event.",
      "output": "Source bound with vanishing normalized volume loss.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-sharp-reference-source"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-quadratic-integration",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-coarse-source",
        "step-sharp-source"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-quadratic-integration",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:04-quadratic\" label=\"lem:04-quadratic\"></span>Let $F$ be a bounded real symmetric operator on $\\mathcal X$, with $F\\ge gI$, $g>0$, and let $F-I$ be trace class. Let $l\\in\\mathcal X$ be real. Suppose that there are a smooth real vector test $b(s)$, odd under endpoint reflection, and a smooth real matrix kernel $\\mathscr C(s,v)$ satisfying $\\mathscr C(s,v)=\\mathscr C(v,s)^{\\mathsf t}$, odd under reflection in either variable, such that $$h_0(l,Q)=\\int b(s)^{\\mathsf t}Q(s)\\,ds,\\qquad\n h_0(Q,(F-I)Q)=\\iint Q(s)^{\\mathsf t}\\mathscr C(s,v)Q(v)\\,ds\\,dv.$$ Let $c\\in\\mathbb R$. For the compensated cost $$V(Q)=c+h_0(l,Q)+\\tfrac12h_0(Q,(F-I)Q),\\qquad\n m_F=c-\\tfrac12h_0(l,F^{-1}l),$$ one has $$\\log\\mathbb E_0e^{-V(Q)}\n \\le -m_F+CL+\\tfrac12\\sum_{\\lambda<1,\\ \\lambda\\in\\operatorname{spec}(F)}\n                    -\\log\\lambda .\n \\label{eq:G-18}$$ Here and below smooth compensated expressions are evaluated on empirical primitives by distributional pairing; their individual singular $h_0$-energies are not defined. For the sharp form used below, assume $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$, $g^{-1}+\\|F\\|+\\|l\\|_{\\mathcal X}\\le CL^a$ for fixed $C,a$, and polynomial physical derivative bounds for $b$ and $\\mathscr C$ in the convention above. Then for every fixed $D$, <a href=\"#eq:G-18\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-18\">[eq:G-18]</a> holds with $CL$ replaced by $o(L)$ and expectation restricted to $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D$. The error is uniform once these bounds and $D$ are fixed. The constant term $c$ is unrestricted: its factor $e^{-c}$ occurs on both sides of the estimate.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Coarse and sharp compensated quadratic integration bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-e-quadratic-integration"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-e-particle-transport",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-particle-transport",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:04-contour\" label=\"lem:04-contour\"></span>Under the hypotheses of part 3 of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:04-integer-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:04-integer-bounds\">29</a>, choose the core projection $P$ of <a href=\"#eq:G-10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:G-10\">[eq:G-10]</a>, with $S=P\\mathcal X$, $R=S^\\perp$, and Schur complement $T_e$. For every prescribed fixed tail exponent, a sufficiently large fixed $D$ gives an exact contour deformation of the reference integral. On $\\|Q\\|_{-2,A}\\le L^D$, its compensated cost is $C_0(Q+a(Q))-h_0(Q,Q)/2+o(1)$, where $$Q+a(Q)=iZ_0+Q_R+T_{SS}^{-1/2}Q_S\n             -T_{SS}^{-1}Pu_z-T_{SS}^{-1}T_{SR}Q_R.$$ The extra Jacobian, after the one-coordinate reference-density cancellation, has modulus $(1+o(1))|\\det T_{SS}|^{-1/2}$. The full cost $\\operatorname{Re}C_0(Q+a(Q))$ has Hessian $I$ on $S$ and $\\operatorname{Re}T_e$ on $R$, and its infimum is at least $m_z$. The complementary contribution is smaller than the prescribed stretched exponential.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-particle-transport",
      "inputs": [

      ],
      "method": "Realize an affine core transformation by configuration-dependent particle displacements, use Stokes’ theorem, and evaluate the reduced Jacobian.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-04-integer-bounds",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-imaginary-trials",
        "step-particle-transport",
        "step-relative-determinant"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-04-integer-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $|\\operatorname{Re}\\eta|\\le a_0<\\pi$ and $|\\operatorname{Im}\\eta|\\le b_0<\\infty$, with the parameter conventions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:03-trial-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:03-trial-bounds\">18</a>. For nonnegative integer dimensions $\\nu_\\sigma$, put $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\n =\\log|e^{i(\\eta+\\pi f+2\\pi j)M}F_\\nu(M)|,\n \\qquad v=\\operatorname{Im}M.$$ The following estimates hold.*\n\n1.  *If $M$ is real and $0\\le\\nu_\\sigma\\le C e^{\\gamma L}$, then, without any restriction on the explicit counts, $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le C_hL+C_hn_e-c_hd_e.$$ This bound is independent of the integer alias $j$.*\n\n\n2.  *Suppose $n_e$ is polynomially bounded, as in part 2 of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:03-trial-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:03-trial-bounds\">18</a>. In the same dimension range, for every real $v$ and integer $j$, $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le C_h L+C_h n_e-c_h d_e-2\\pi jv\n                     +C_h(v^2+(1+f)|v|).\n     \\label{eq:G-15}$$*\n\n\n\n3.  *Assume the common hypotheses of parts 3 and 4 of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:03-trial-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:03-trial-bounds\">18</a>, polynomial bounds on $n_e,|j|,|M|,\\ell_e$, and $\\nu_\\sigma\\asymp m$. Choose the imaginary imbalance and trial density from part 3 of that proposition. Then $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le C_h L+C_h n_e-c_h d_e-c_\\delta U^2 L+o(L).\n     \\label{eq:G-16}$$ If $U\\le U_0$, the choice from part 4 instead gives $$\\mathcal L_{\\nu,j}(M)\\le\\frac{3\\gamma L}{8}\n     -2L\\frac{\\operatorname{Re}(\\eta^2)}{4\\kappa}\n     +C\\varepsilon L+o(L)+C_h n_e-c_{h,\\varepsilon}d_e.\n     \\label{eq:G-17}$$ Here $\\delta$ can be taken sufficiently small without degrading the displayed damping and per-particle constants, and $U_0$ may then be fixed arbitrarily, as in part 4 of that proposition.*\n\n*The estimates are uniform once the parameters in the trial bounds and the polynomial size powers are fixed.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Uniform bounds for integer-dimensional electric sectors.",

The degree and coefficient bounds are fixed before any endpoint expansion.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-04-interpolation",

For every fixed $b>0$ there is a jointly holomorphic function $\\widehat F(\\nu,M)$, with $\\nu=(\\nu_+,\\nu_-)\\in\\mathbb C^2$, on a neighborhood of the indicated $M$-disk and the polydisk $|\\nu_\\sigma-m|\\le\\sqrt m$, such that $$|\\widehat F(\\nu,M)|\\le \\exp(L^C),\\qquad\n |\\widehat F(\\nu,M)-F_\\nu(M)|\\le e^{-L^b}\n       \\quad\\text{for }\\nu\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^2\\text{ in the polydisk}.\n \\label{eq:G-20}$$ Here $C$ is fixed after the truncation bounds and $b$ are fixed. The bounds are uniform in the real explicit-particle positions on their labeled lines. The integrals include their reference masses $J_{\\nu_+}(w_0)J_{\\nu_-}(w_0)$, but no external phase factor.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-e-cylinder-exponent",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:04-cylinder-exponent\" label=\"thm:04-cylinder-exponent\"></span>For every fixed $\\chi>0$, along even $N$, $$Z_N(\\chi)=N^{\\tau(\\chi)+o(1)},\\qquad\n \\tau(\\chi)=\n \\begin{cases}\n \\displaystyle\\frac16-\\frac{2}{3\\pi^2}\n              \\bigl(\\arccos(\\chi/2)\\bigr)^2,&0<\\chi\\le2,\\\\[4pt]\n \\displaystyle\\frac16+\\frac{2}{3\\pi^2}\n              \\bigl(\\operatorname{arcosh}(\\chi/2)\\bigr)^2,&\\chi\\ge2.\n \\end{cases}$$",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-05-winding-data",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-physical-vacuum",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-winding-data",
          "exact_quote": "The function $\\Lambda(\\chi;z)$ is rational in $e^{2iz}$, with denominator dividing $\\prod_i\\mathscr D(z-b_i)$, where $\\mathscr D$ is the local denominator in <a href=\"#eq:E-local-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:E-local-weights\">[eq:E-local-weights]</a>, and tends to $1+\\chi$ at both ends of this multiplicative coordinate. At the balanced specialization it is invariant under $z\\mathrel{%\n  \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%\n  \\mapsto%\n  \\EndAccSupp{}%\n}3\\lambda-z$. For $u=b_i$ and generic remaining sites, $$\\Lambda(\\chi;u+j\\lambda)\\Lambda(\\chi;u)\n =\\begin{cases}\n   \\Lambda(\\chi;u+\\lambda),&j=2,\\\\\n   1,&j=3.\n  \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:05-annular-pinch}$$ Set $w=2i(z-z_0)$ and, with derivatives evaluated at $\\chi=0$, define $$\\begin{gathered}\n f(w)=\\partial_\\chi\\Lambda(\\chi;z),\\qquad\n h_2(w)=\\partial_\\chi^2\\Lambda(\\chi;z),\\qquad\n g(w)=h_2(w)-f(w)^2,\\\\\n (\\mathscr L v)(w)=v(w+i\\beta)+v(w-i\\beta)-v(w).\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:05-pressure-derivatives}$$ Then $\\mathscr L f$ and $\\mathscr L g$ vanish to orders $2m,m,m$ at $0,i\\beta,-i\\beta$, respectively. The functions $f-1$ and $h_2$ are even rational functions of $e^w$, vanish at both real infinities, and have poles only at the translates of these three knots by $i\\pi$, modulo $2\\pi i$, with the same respective order bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-annular-data",
      "inputs": [
        "Refined marked-region states",
        "Local splitter identities",
        "Simple annular eigenline"
      ],
      "method": "Fuse annular auxiliaries and differentiate the pinch relations before confluence.",
      "output": "Rational pressure data and prescribed jets.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-05-winding-data"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-05-hardy-interpolation",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-hardy-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $0<a<1$, the operators $D_a$ are bounded and satisfy $$\\operatorname{Re}D_a(k)\\ge c_a>0\n \\quad(k\\in\\mathbb R),\\qquad\n \\|(P_ND_a|_{P_NH})^{-1}\\|\\le c_a^{-1}.\n \\label{eq:05-coercivity}$$ Moreover, writing $\\|\\cdot\\|_{\\partial,p}$ for the sum of the two boundary $L^p$ norms, one has $$\\|e^{aw}(1-f)\\|_{L^p(\\operatorname{Im}w=y)}\n       \\le C_a e^{aA_N},\n \\qquad p=2,4,\\quad |y|\\le h+\\beta.\n \\label{eq:05-first-derivative-norms}$$ For fixed $0<a<1/2$, $$\\bigl|\\mathscr Lf(s)\\bigr|+\n \\bigl|\\mathscr Lg(s)\\bigr|\n \\le C_a|B_N(s)|\\bigl(1+e^{2a(A_N-s)}\\bigr),\n \\qquad s\\ge0.\n \\label{eq:05-jet-residual}$$ For $\\mathscr Lf$ alone, $2a$ can be replaced by any fixed $a\\in(0,1)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-hardy-interpolation",
      "inputs": [
        "Annular jet and pole data",
        "Hardy model space"

      "method": "Realize rational partial fractions as a coercive compressed multiplier and divide residuals by the prescribed inner function.",
      "output": "Uniform interpolation inverse and boundary and residual bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-05-hardy-interpolation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-05-pressure-bounds",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-pressure-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $0<c<C<\\infty$ such that, for all sufficiently large even $N$, $$\\frac cN\\le f(0)\\le\\frac CN,\n \\qquad |g(0)|\\le\\frac CN.\n \\label{eq:05-winding-pressure}$$ In fact $e^{\\gamma A_N}f(0)$ has a finite strictly positive limit.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-05-winding-suppression",
          "exact_quote": "On the balanced cylinder with period $V=iN\\sqrt3/2$, let $[\\ell]$ range over simple essential polygons modulo integer horizontal translations, and set $W(\\ell)=\\rho^{|\\ell|}$. If $d_x(\\ell)$ is their horizontal span, then $$\\sum_{[\\ell]}W(\\ell)\\asymp N^{-1},\\qquad\n \\sum_{[\\ell]}W(\\ell)d_x(\\ell)\\le C.\n \\label{eq:05-winding-moment}$$ The total weight of essential polygons separating the two marked points in the balanced two-marker geometry is bounded uniformly in $N$. At each fixed nonnegative fugacity, deleting all these polygons changes its partition function by at most a bounded multiplicative factor.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-variable-algebra",
          "exact_quote": "With $\\widehat P=P_{x+\\lambda}$ for $j=2$, and the identity for $j=3$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n R_yR_{-y}=I,\\qquad P_{j\\lambda}=S_jS_j^{\\mathsf t},\\\\\n (P_x)_2(P_{x+j\\lambda})_1(I\\otimes S_j)\n   =d_j(x)(S_j\\otimes I)\\widehat P,\\qquad\n d_j(x)=1+nq_j\\frac{A_{x+j\\lambda}U_x}\n                          {e_{x+j\\lambda}e_x}.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:05-variable-splitting}$$ The raw boxes also satisfy the three-box Yang–Baxter identity.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-05-open-pinch",
          "exact_quote": "The matrices $t(z)$ commute and are invariant under $z\\leftrightarrow\\bar z$. At $\\lambda=\\lambda_0$, $x_i=\\lambda/2$, and $z=3\\lambda/2$, this is the transfer of two physical columns with vacant auxiliary boundaries. Its simple eigenvalue continuing $1$ will be denoted by $\\Lambda_o(n;z)$. It is rational and $\\pi$-periodic in $z$, equals one at $n=0$, and tends to $1+n$ at both imaginary infinities. For $u=x_i$, $v=u+j\\lambda$, its pinch rule is $$\\Lambda_o(n;v)\\Lambda_o(n;u)\n =c_bc_t\\prod_kd_j(u-x_k)d_j(\\bar v-x_k)\n \\begin{cases}\n \\Lambda_o(n;u+\\lambda),&j=2,\\\\\n 1,&j=3,\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:05-open-pinch}$$ where $w_0=u+v-3\\lambda$ and $$c_b=\\frac{e_{w_0}+nq_j\\mathfrak m(\\bar u)A_{w_0}}{L_{w_0}},\n \\qquad\n c_t=\\frac{e_{-w_0}+nq_j\\mathfrak m(v)A_{-w_0}}{L_{-w_0}}.\n \\label{eq:05-boundary-scalars}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.3"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-open-transfer-pinches",
      "inputs": [
        "Variable local identities",
        "Open-strip spectral simplicity"

      "method": "Exchange auxiliary lines through boundary tensors and fuse descending pairs with the explicit boundary scalars.",

    },

          "source_id": "PM-237.3"

          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:05-planar-tail\" label=\"thm:05-planar-tail\"></span>Uniformly for $R\\ge0$, $$\\sum_{[P]:\\,\\operatorname{diam}(P)>R}\\rho^{|P|}\n       \\le\\frac{C}{(1+R)^2}.\n \\label{eq:05-planar-tail}$$ The sum is over unrooted, unoriented polygons modulo triangular-lattice translations.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-same-group-string-exponents**: For same-group sites, multiplication of the formal string interactions gives the prescribed residue exponent array, including negative exponents.
- **invariant-cross-group-string-exponents**: For different groups, ordering the plus group first gives the prescribed cross-group residue exponent array.
- **invariant-residue-multiplicity**: Residue entries retain their signed multiplicities; the zero vector and the unit vector at residue zero are distinct.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-certify-cylinder-asymptotic**: Treat agreement of the finite exponent arrays as verification of the cylinder growth exponent. -> The check establishes a finite algebraic ingredient and does not evaluate the contour integrals or their asymptotic normalization.
- **anti-evaluate-colliding-factors**: Use an exponent array to evaluate individual singular factors at colliding site arguments. -> Combining formal exponents does not implement the continuation required for the complete finite cylinder expression.
- **anti-erase-negative-exponents**: Replace signed exponent entries by unsigned occurrence counts. -> Denominator factors become numerator factors and the displayed same-group arrays are changed.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The formal site-string construction gives a finite exact computation: expand the Laurent polynomial defining the mutual interaction exponents and compare every allowed label pair with the explicitly displayed arrays. This checks that algebraic construction only; the cylinder and nesting asymptotics remain outside the executable certificate.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with group ('same' or 'cross') and integer labels l, k in {-1, 0, 1}.
# PRECONDITIONS: The plus group is first; same means both groups are plus, cross means plus then minus.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of eight signed integer residue exponents, ordered by residues zero through seven.
# TIME: Bounded exact sparse polynomial arithmetic for the prescribed finite label domain.
# SPACE: Bounded sparse integer coefficient dictionaries and an eight-entry output list.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python integers; Laurent exponents are integers.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys

def add(*polys):
    out = {}
    for poly in polys:
        for power, coefficient in poly.items():
            out[power] = out.get(power, 0) + coefficient
    return {power: coefficient for power, coefficient in out.items() if coefficient}

def scale(poly, factor):
    return {power: factor * coefficient for power, coefficient in poly.items()}

def multiply(left, right):
    out = {}
    for p, a in left.items():
        for q, b in right.items():
            out[p + q] = out.get(p + q, 0) + a * b
    return {power: coefficient for power, coefficient in out.items() if coefficient}

def exponents(request):
    if not isinstance(request, dict) or set(request) != {'group', 'l', 'k'}:
        raise ValueError('invalid input fields')
    label = request['l']
    other = request['k']
    if type(label) is not int or type(other) is not int or label not in (-1, 0, 1) or other not in (-1, 0, 1):
        raise ValueError('invalid labels')
    if request['group'] == 'same':
        omega = 1
        B = {2: 1, -2: 1, 3: 1, -3: 1}
        F = {1: 1, -1: 1}
        E = {0: 2, 2: 1, -2: 1, 1: -1, -1: -1}
    elif request['group'] == 'cross':
        omega = -1
        B = {2: 2, 3: 2}
        F = {1: 2}
        E = {0: 2, 2: 2, 1: -2}
    else:
        raise ValueError('invalid group')
    a = 1 - label
    b = 1 - omega * other
    A = {h: 1 for h in range(1, a + 1)}
    D = {-omega * h: 1 for h in range(1, b + 1)}
    polynomial = add(B, scale(multiply(F, add(A, D)), -1), multiply(E, multiply(A, D)))
    result = [0] * 8
    for power, coefficient in polynomial.items():
        residue = (-power) % 8
        result[residue] += coefficient
    return result

print(json.dumps(exponents(json.load(sys.stdin)), separators=(',', ':')))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cylinder exponent describes asymptotic partition growth but supplies no application interface or finite-size error certificate for general software validation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The contour representation uses signed particle activities and complex phases; it does not construct a positive sampling procedure for game or simulation dynamics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The dimension interpolation establishes existence and approximation bounds under fixed polynomial restrictions, without a numerical evaluation procedure or explicit precision budget for a reusable computational tool. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite cylinder formula gives a concrete symbolic evaluation construction: enumerate the site labels satisfying the equal group-sum constraint, form their prescribed weights, sum them, and divide by the scalar normalization where it is nonzero. This construction evaluates the finite algebraic expression; it supplies no efficiency guarantee. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The annular transfer has a concrete finite connectivity representation: a cyclic noncrossing partial matching together with a marked complementary region. The marked region retains the information needed to distinguish essential cycles from contractible cycles during composition. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The smooth core projection approximates an interaction operator in trace norm. Its existence and regularity bounds do not specify a hardware mapping, parallel execution schedule, or accelerator resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local identities concern dilute-loop transfer boxes, splitting maps, and closed-cycle weights. They establish no cryptographic primitive, hardness assumption, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reference source inequality controls linear statistics under specified independent reference ensembles. It does not establish learning guarantees for fitted models or arbitrary training-data distributions. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The polygon-count estimate bounds unnormalized coefficient masses with unspecified constants. It does not provide an objective optimizer, a feasible-solution construction, or a computable pruning threshold for an enumeration search. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Exponential slab decay concerns path propagation through a fixed geometric cylinder. It supplies no communication protocol, failure model, or consistency guarantee for distributed processes. |
| other (Mathematical statistical mechanics) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs an exact finite partition representation for disjoint separating polygons and identifies its physical balanced-cylinder specialization. The label sum and scalar normalization provide a concrete algebraic construction for this lattice observable, while the asymptotic theorems determine its growth regime. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-critical-honeycomb-model** (hypothesis): The physical results concern the regular honeycomb lattice with the prescribed critical vertex activity. They do not establish the same exponents for other lattices or other vertex activities. Blocked use: ['Transferring the exponent to an arbitrary planar graph.', 'Applying the critical exponent after changing the vertex weight.']
- **limit-balanced-marker-geometry** (hypothesis): The cylinder exponent uses the prescribed balanced marker placement and even circumference. Arbitrary marker displacements are outside that assertion. Blocked use: ['Applying the cylinder exponent to odd circumference.', 'Replacing the prescribed markers by arbitrary positions without a separate comparison.']
- **limit-simple-disjoint-separating-polygons** (hypothesis): The partition counts simple polygons that are pairwise vertex-disjoint and separate the markers. Its definition includes the empty family. Blocked use: ['Counting intersecting or self-intersecting loops under the same formula.', 'Dropping the empty configuration.', 'Replacing marker separation by winding alone.']
- **limit-fixed-fugacity-asymptotic** (formal_scope): The cylinder exponent is a fixed-positive-fugacity asymptotic. It supplies neither an exact finite-size amplitude nor a uniform error estimate over a growing fugacity range. Blocked use: ['Treating the exponent formula as an exact finite-size partition value.', 'Deriving an unbounded-fugacity estimate solely from the fixed-parameter asymptotic.']
- **limit-fixed-width-decay** (formal_scope): The slab-decay constants depend on the fixed cylinder. The statement does not provide a decay rate uniform in growing circumference. Blocked use: ['Using the fixed-cylinder decay rate unchanged while increasing circumference.']
- **limit-algebraic-versus-physical-vacuum** (hypothesis): Rational stationarity with zero closed-loop weight must be distinguished from the convergent physical half-cylinder interpretation, which is established at the physical row parameters. Blocked use: ['Interpreting a generic complex stationary vector as a positive configuration law.', 'Assigning nonzero closed-loop weight while retaining the zero-loop stationarity argument.']
- **limit-finite-label-collisions** (edge_case): The finite label formula requires the stated fugacity parameterization and a nonzero auxiliary parameter. Apparent collision singularities are resolved by continuation of the expression. Blocked use: ['Evaluating singular individual summands by direct substitution at colliding sites.', 'Dividing by a scalar normalization whose nonvanishing has not been established.']
- **limit-contour-species-are-signed** (formal_scope): The contour construction is a signed representation with fused constituents and coefficientwise cancellations. It is not a positive particle sampling law. Blocked use: ['Using negative far activities as transition probabilities.', 'Separating fused constituents before checking poles or signs.', 'Replacing the finite activity truncation by an asserted convergent infinite series.']
- **limit-balanced-denominator-only** (formal_scope): The denominator asymptotic is proved for equal group sizes at the prescribed confluent sites. The unequal-size contour identity does not extend that asymptotic uniformly. Blocked use: ['Using the balanced denominator estimate for arbitrary group imbalance.']
- **limit-neutrality-and-energy-domain** (hypothesis): The coercivity comparison assumes real weighted-total-neutral fluctuations and is extended in the stated energy topology. Arbitrary nonneutral fields are outside this comparison. Blocked use: ['Applying the coercivity norm equivalence to a fluctuation with nonzero weighted total mass.', 'Treating an empirical point configuration as a smooth finite-self-energy density.']
- **limit-compensated-sector-admissibility** (hypothesis): The physical compensated sector uses an integer imbalance with nonnegative interior particle dimensions and retains the unnormalized reference measures and explicit factorials. Blocked use: ['Including a sector with negative interior dimension.', 'Replacing an unnormalized reference integral by its normalized expectation without restoring its mass.', 'Dropping the species factorial divisors.']
- **limit-compensated-empirical-pairing** (formal_scope): The compensated observable is a smooth-kernel pairing. Its empirical evaluation cannot be implemented as subtraction of separately divergent self energies. Blocked use: ['Evaluating the singular true and reference empirical self energies separately and subtracting them.']
- **limit-trial-fields-and-parameter-order** (hypothesis): The sharp tilt bounds require the polynomial restrictions, coordinate regularity, bounded end phases, and ordered choices of fixed parameters stated in the trial proposition. The untruncated real bound has a broader regime. Blocked use: ['Applying the sharp tilt estimate to unrestricted explicit counts or aliases.', 'Letting smoothing and wall parameters vary with size without maintaining the stated order of limits.', 'Treating a variational density as an already realized particle contour.']
- **limit-regularity-needs-polynomial-data** (hypothesis): The compensated source bounds assume fixed polynomial bounds on imbalance, explicit count, and excursion. Core analytic continuation is distinct from smooth endpoint reflection. Blocked use: ['Claiming the same source bounds for unrestricted explicit data.', 'Assuming analyticity through the folded endpoints from smooth reflection alone.']
- **limit-core-iterated-limit** (approximation): The core tolerance is fixed before the large-size limit. The Schur comparison error vanishes after the subsequent tolerance limit and is not a vanishing normalized error at fixed tolerance. Blocked use: ['Reporting the core determinant error as negligible at fixed tolerance.', 'Selecting a size-dependent tolerance without proving uniformity of the construction.']
- **limit-sharp-reference-event** (hypothesis): The sharp source estimate requires comparable reference dimensions, polynomial physical derivative bounds, and restriction to the controlled empirical-primitive event. The coarse estimate retains a volume loss. Blocked use: ['Removing the event restriction from the sharp source inequality.', 'Applying the sharp bound to arbitrary dimension or test families.', 'Discarding reference normalization masses in the physical partition.']
- **limit-positive-quadratic-integration** (hypothesis): Quadratic integration requires a positive gap, a trace-class perturbation, and smooth distributional representations of the linear and quadratic terms. The sharper form has additional polynomial bounds. Blocked use: ['Applying the determinant correction to an indefinite quadratic operator.', 'Dropping the positive part before completing the square.', 'Using the sharp form without its smoothness and controlled-event assumptions.']
- **limit-particle-transport-scope** (formal_scope): Particle transport deforms a finite integration chain under the sharp sector hypotheses. It does not assert a pushforward density on the real line for the complex contour. Blocked use: ['Treating the complex displacement as a real probability transport.', 'Assuming the particle-dependent map is globally injective or holomorphic.']
- **limit-dimension-interpolation-is-approximation** (approximation): Dimension continuation is a holomorphic approximation to integer-dimensional integrals on the specified polydisk. It is not an integral with noninteger particle number. Blocked use: ['Interpreting a complex dimension as an actual particle count.', 'Using the integer-grid error bound as a certified approximation outside the stated domain.']
- **limit-grid-transfer-majorant** (hypothesis): Grid transfer needs a sufficiently non-negligible grid majorant and a target at polynomial distance from the central dimension. Coarse holomorphic growth alone does not supply the sharp transferred bound. Blocked use: ['Transferring an arbitrarily tiny grid bound without the prescribed precision margin.', 'Evaluating at a target outside the polynomial neighborhood and claiming the same loss.']
- **limit-complex-upper-bound-not-sector-lower-bound** (formal_scope): The complex strip estimate is an upper bound. The matching positive-fugacity exponent uses propagation from the empty-configuration value and coefficient positivity, rather than a lower bound on an individual complex sector. Blocked use: ['Claiming pointwise lower saturation throughout the complex strip.', 'Using a lower estimate for a single oscillatory sector as if it were proved.']
- **limit-count-masses-not-probabilities** (formal_scope): The polygon-count estimate bounds unnormalized coefficient masses. A probability tail or a computable discarded-mass tolerance requires normalization and additional quantitative control. Blocked use: ['Reading a coefficient bound directly as a count probability.', 'Claiming a certified numerical truncation threshold from unspecified constants.']
- **limit-pressure-observables-and-translation-classes** (formal_scope): Winding pressure weights essential loops and discards contractible loops. Open-strip pressure counts planar shapes modulo the full triangular translation lattice with vertical placement multiplicity. These conventions differ from the separating-marker partition. Blocked use: ['Substituting the essential-loop pressure partition for the separating-marker observable.', 'Mixing horizontal translation classes with full planar translation classes.', "Omitting vertical placements or the transfer's column normalization in the pressure interpretation."]
- **limit-planar-tail-unrooted-shapes** (formal_scope): The planar diameter tail is a bound for unrooted, unoriented polygon translation classes. It is not directly a bound for all rooted placements enclosing a prescribed point. Blocked use: ['Applying the shape tail to rooted polygons without counting placements.', 'Ignoring enclosure and strip geometry in macroscopic removal.']
- **limit-nesting-bounded-factor-comparison** (hypothesis): The planar comparison requires fixed nonnegative fugacity and sufficiently small fixed neighborhoods. The exponent conclusion requires positive fugacity, and the diameter cutoff applies separately to every polygon. Blocked use: ['Replacing the bounded-factor comparison by an exact factorization.', 'Applying it to overlapping marker neighborhoods.', 'Interpreting the cutoff as a bound only on the combined nest.']
- **limit-length-observable-and-conditioning** (hypothesis): The length–nest comparison uses winding-weighted ordered boundary paths in the stated convex-domain or homogeneous-strip setting. The raw first-length bound is unnormalized; the conditional exponent requires the specified penetration event and normalized critical weights. Blocked use: ['Applying the conditional exponent to unrestricted paths.', 'Replacing a raw first-length upper bound by an unconditional expected-length asymptotic.', 'Removing convexity while retaining the positive winding comparison.']
- **limit-finite-algebra-certificate** (formal_scope): A finite check of the formal string exponent arrays verifies that algebraic ingredient only. It does not certify the contour integrals, limiting determinants, pressure estimates, or nesting and length exponents. Blocked use: ["Promoting exhaustive agreement of the finite arrays to verification of the paper's asymptotic theorems."]

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We determine the growth exponent, at every fixed positive loop fugacity, of the critical honeycomb partition function for disjoint polygons separating two prescribed markers on a balanced cylinder. At fugacity two the cylinder exponent is 1/6; the corresponding planar nesting exponent and middle-strip nesting exponent are 1/12.
